## Manuscript
"Wandering spleen in children: a novel splenopexy technique" (Jun Shu, Jun Yang [corresponding], Hongqiang Bian, Fei Peng, Ji Wang — Wuhan Children's Hospital, Tongji Medical College, HUST).

- Original 4-case submission: `Wandering spleen in children A novel splenopexy technique.docx`
- Revised 5-case version (adds Patient 5, updates follow-up): `Wandering spleen in children A novel splenopexy technique - 5 cases revised.docx`
- **EJP Original Article (the version to submit): `Wandering spleen in children - EJP original article.docx`** — 2477 words, Table 1 (per encounter), 5 figures, 20 references, structured abstract, What is Known/What is New box
- EJP Brief Report (fallback if the editor asks to downgrade): `Wandering spleen in children - EJP brief report.docx` — 1188 words, 1 table, 1 figure, 12 references
- **Cover letter (EJP submission): `Cover letter - EJP submission.docx`** — standard cover letter to the Editor-in-Chief, addressed 22 Sep 2026, summarising the paper's two contributions (delay–splenic loss association; preliminary two-pole technique), originality/no-conflicts/funding/ethics declarations. Does not mention the prior IJS submission or rejection — standard practice, not something journals expect disclosed unless asked.

## Submission history
- **International Journal of Surgery** (IJS-D-26-02226): rejected 21 Sep 2026 after ~7-month review. Reviewers #1/#2 positive; Reviewer #3 gave "Major revision" and flagged: overstated efficacy of the reinforced-fixation technique (n=1, 6-month follow-up), wrong manuscript type (filed as Quality Improvement Study, is actually a retrospective case series), and heterogeneous outcomes (3/4 original patients lost the spleen). IJS offered an automatic transfer to Annals of Medicine and Surgery / Medicine — **declined**: IJS and AMSU are both part of the Riaz Agha journal group currently under a Clarivate citation-manipulation hold (Retraction Watch, Mar 2026); not pursuing either.

## Target journal decision
**Decided: submit to European Journal of Pediatrics** (Springer). IF 2.9, JCR Q1; Aims & Scope explicitly lists "pediatric surgery" as a covered field. Manuscript types are original articles/reviews/mini-reviews/brief reports — no dedicated case-series category, so the case-history sections should be tightened toward a brief-report style before submission.

Other journals evaluated and set aside: Journal of Pediatric Surgery (Q2, IF 2.3 — good fallback), BMC Pediatrics / Frontiers in Pediatrics–Pediatric Surgery section (Q2, ~1.9–2.2 — good fallback), Surgical Endoscopy (Q1, IF ~2.7 — stretch, scope less specific), Frontiers in Medicine (Q1, IF 3.6 — rejected, no pediatric-surgery section, poor scope fit), The Journal of Pediatrics (Q1, IF 3.6 — rejected, general-pediatrics scope, ~15% acceptance rate, Brief Report format capped at 1000 words/2 illustrations which would gut the case detail).

## Article type — decided
**Original Article.** EJP allows 3000 words and ~6 tables/figures (excess to supplementary); Brief Report allows only 1500 words, one table and/or figure, and 12 references. The figures are the paper's strongest asset (praised by IJS Reviewer #1), the rare-disease value lies in the longitudinal detail, and an editor can downgrade a submission to Brief Report but never upgrade one. The Brief Report version is kept as the fallback.

Both EJP versions are reframed around **delay, not technique, as the determinant of splenic loss**, with the reinforced two-pole fixation presented as a preliminary technical description. This answers IJS Reviewer #3's two heaviest criticisms directly.

## Data corrections applied
- **Case 3's interval from first presentation to splenic surgery is 62 months** (4.5 → 9.67 years), not 36 months. The "36 months" in the original case history is the gap between her *second* and *third* encounters and is correct there. The three-case mean of 32 months (16 + 19 + 62) is unchanged.

## Title — decided (both EJP versions)
**"Wandering spleen in children: delay determines splenic loss and a reinforced two-pole splenopexy technique"** (no comma before "and" — author asked to remove it) — author wanted the technique named in the title; combined with the delay finding for search visibility and impact. Applied to both `EJP original article.docx` and `EJP brief report.docx`.

## Case 5 imaging — first round rejected, second round applied (22 Sep 2026)
Author's first two candidate whirl-sign CT photos (phone photos of a screen — not PACS exports) were reviewed and rejected: neither clearly showed a diagnostic whirl sign (one was a low pelvic slice showing the ectopic spleen mass below the pedicle level; the other showed indeterminate stranding, not a clear spiral vessel pattern).

Author then supplied a second pair: an axial slice at a low/sacral level, and a coronal reconstruction showing bilateral hip joints, dilated bowel loops, and a swirled soft-tissue structure centrally. Claude's read: the coronal image's swirled structure sits in the right anatomical position to represent the twisted splenic vessels wrapping around the splenic flexure of the colon — matching the Results text's existing description ("splenic vessels encircling the splenic flexure of the colon") — more convincingly than the axial candidate, which looked more like generic perisplenic fat stranding. Flagged to the author that this read is not radiologist-confirmed and asked whether the radiology report specifies the diagnostic slice; author replied by choosing the coronal image without further comment on the report.

**Applied to the Original Article** (per the swap plan already agreed): Fig. 1 (Case 2 infarct CT — the most replaceable image, since Table 1 already carries that data) replaced with the new Case 5 whirl-sign coronal image. The Case 2 sentence in Results lost its "(Fig. 1)" citation (text otherwise unchanged); the Case 5 paragraph gained "(Fig. 1)" after "a whirl sign formed by the spiralling splenic vessels." No other figure needed renumbering — Fig. 1 was the only citation before Case 5's paragraph in reading order, so Figs. 2–5 (Case 3 ×3, technique schematic) kept their numbers unchanged. New Fig. 1 legend: "Coronal CT in Case 5, showing a whirl sign formed by the twisted splenic vessels encircling the splenic flexure of the colon, with the spleen displaced into the pelvis" — deliberately has no "(arrow)"/"(star)" pointer, since the supplied image carries no annotation overlay (unlike Figs. 2–4, which do). Re-verified after rebuild: figure citations run 1→5 in sequential first-appearance order, reference citations still 1→20 with no orphans/out-of-range, word count unchanged (2614/3000).

**Not applied to the Brief Report** — its single figure slot is already used for the technique schematic, and EJP's Brief Report format caps it at one table and/or figure total, so there is no room to add a second image there; this was never part of the swap plan.

**Resolution caveat still applies.** The new Fig. 1 image is 568×683 px natively — the same sub-print-quality range already flagged for Figs. 2–4 (formerly Figs. 1–4). It still needs a proper de-identified PACS export before submission; using it as-is at print size would look same as the current low-resolution figures.

**Whirl-sign finding now confirmed by the actual radiology report (22 Sep 2026).** Author supplied Case 5's official CT report (Wuhan Women and Children Medical and Health Care Center, non-contrast abdominal/pelvic CT). Findings text matches the manuscript's Case 5 CT description essentially line-for-line, including the whirl sign ("旋涡状" vessel pattern) and — most importantly — "脾脏血管缠绕结肠脾曲" (splenic vessels encircling the splenic flexure of the colon), which is the exact finding the chosen coronal image was picked to illustrate. Impression: 游走脾伴脾扭转 (wandering spleen with splenic torsion), 脾大、密度不均 (enlarged, heterogeneous spleen), consistent with the manuscript throughout. No manuscript text changes needed — the existing Results paragraph is already accurate to this report. The report itself is not committed to the repository (carries the patient's name and exam ID); only the diagnostic content is recorded here. This resolves the "not radiologist-confirmed" caveat for the whirl-sign finding itself — the resolution/PACS-export caveat above is separate and still open.

## Self-review and fixes applied (21 Sep 2026)
Claude reviewed both EJP versions as a strict peer reviewer against 9 criteria (novelty, title/abstract/body/conclusion consistency, design and inclusion criteria, statistics, results-vs-conclusions, overclaiming/causal misuse, table-figure-text consistency, academic writing norms, rejection risks) and classified findings as Major/General/Language. The author asked for the 4 Major (must-fix) text-level items to be applied to **both** `EJP original article.docx` and `EJP brief report.docx`:

1. **Causal language softened throughout** ("delay determines splenic loss" / "tracked delay" / "the determinant of" / "lost to delay" → "was associated with delay") — title, abstract, Discussion opening line, and full Conclusion in both versions. The neutral research-question use of "determines" in the Introduction ("what determines whether the spleen is saved") was left as-is — it poses a question, it doesn't assert an answer.
2. **Limitations paragraph now explicitly names the causal-inference limitation**: the cohort only includes children who ultimately had surgery (children diagnosed but never operated on are not captured), n=5 with no statistical comparison, so confounding cannot be excluded — this was previously entirely missing; the old Limitations paragraph only discussed the technique's limitations (n=1, single-centre), never the delay-causation claim's.
3. **Removed the unsupported "adds little operative time" / "It is quick" claim** — no operative-time data exists in the manuscript for the technique itself (only Case 5's splenectomy has a timed value), so this was an unevidenced assertion.
2/3 combined also fixed **the internal contradiction** where the Discussion asserted the technique is "in principle independent of splenic size" and then, two sentences later (Original Article) / next paragraph (Brief Report), admitted it is completely untested in the giant spleen that motivated it. The size-independence claim is now hedged at first mention with a forward pointer ("... although this remains to be confirmed (see below)").

New title (both versions): **"Wandering spleen in children: splenic loss is associated with delay rather than technique, and a reinforced two-pole splenopexy"**.

Word counts after fixes: Original Article 2549/3000; Brief Report 1247/1500 — both still comfortably under their EJP limits.

## General-tier review items — also applied (21 Sep 2026)
Author asked for these to be done too. Applied to both versions unless noted:
- **Case 5 vs Cases 1–3 "same mechanism" wording** (Original Article Discussion only — Brief Report's Discussion didn't make this claim to begin with): now distinguishes "diagnostic [delay]" (Case 5, misdiagnosed elsewhere) from "a deferred decision to operate" (Cases 1–3, family declined a correct diagnosis), rather than asserting they were the same mechanism.
- **Case 5's follow-up duration now stated in the Results running text**, not just in Table 1: Original Article adds "He remains well at the most recent follow-up, 5 months after operation" to the Case 5 paragraph; Brief Report adds "; he remains well 5 months later" to the same sentence.
- **Wandering spleen now explicitly defined in Methods** in both versions ("an abnormally mobile spleen displaced from its normal fossa on ultrasonography or computed tomography, with or without torsion of the vascular pedicle" / similar wording).
- **Thrombocytosis-timing mechanism reframed as an explicit hypothesis** (Original Article only — Brief Report's existing "appears to reflect" phrasing was already adequately hedged and was left unchanged): "The most plausible explanation is... Reactive thrombocytosis requires..." → "We hypothesize that... is thought to require... may mark...".
- Incidental fix while inserting the Brief Report's Methods definition: spelled out "computed tomography" instead of the bare abbreviation "CT", since Brief Report's Introduction never defines CT before Methods (Original Article's Introduction does, so no equivalent issue there).

Word counts after this round: Original Article 2609/3000 (391 words of headroom); Brief Report 1277/1500 (223 words of headroom).

## Citation-correspondence audit and fixes (21 Sep 2026)
Claude did a full in-text-citation ↔ reference-list audit of the Original Article (all 20 refs, no orphans, no out-of-range numbers, sequential first-appearance order confirmed programmatically) and flagged 3 priority items. Author asked Claude to look up the actual papers (via web search, not title-guessing) and fix them. Findings, verified against real abstracts:

1. **Okazaki et al. 2010** is a **single case report** (one 4-year-old girl, laparoscopic splenopexy+gastropexy for gastric-volvulus-associated wandering spleen) — confirmed NOT a "larger multicentre experience," so citing it for that claim was wrong. Its real content is a strong match for the Introduction's gastric-volvulus/pancreatic-tail/hollow-viscera sentence instead (exactly what the Brief Report already, independently, had it doing correctly).
2. **Alqadi & Saxena 2019** is a systematic review pooling **20 children from 15 articles (1998–2016)**, concluding laparoscopic splenopexy is feasible **"with no reported conversions or complications."** This directly contradicts the Brief Report's old citation of it (alongside Zivkovic) to support "some authors have proceeded to splenectomy" when the pouch is too small.
3. **Wang et al. 2022** (thrombocytosis paper) and **Zivkovic 1998** (sutureless button-and-hole technique) were both confirmed, via their real abstracts, to have nothing to do with pouch-size/conversion-to-splenectomy — the Original Article's citation of Wang there, and the Brief Report's citation of both there, were simple mismatches.

**Fixes applied:**
- **Original Article**: renumbered the full reference list (Okazaki moved from position 14 to its new position 8, where it's now correctly cited in the Introduction; Radillo through Zivkovic shifted from 8–13 to 9–14; positions 1–7 and 15–20 unchanged). The "asymptomatic splenopexy" sentence now cites Fiquet-Francois (multicentre) + Alqadi (pooled review) instead of Fiquet-Francois + Okazaki. The Introduction's technique-overview sentence now explicitly names "a sutureless button-and-hole fixation" as a fourth technique so its citation range legitimately covers Zivkovic. The false "pouch too small → splenectomy [Wang]" citation was removed and replaced with a pointer to the paper's own Case 3, which is discussed in the very next paragraph and is a real, accurate illustration of exactly that scenario. Verified programmatically after the edit: all 20 references cited, no orphans, strictly ascending first-appearance order (1→20) preserved.
- **Brief Report**: removed the same false "[5, 9]" citation from the equivalent sentence (Alqadi's review explicitly contradicts the claim; Zivkovic is unrelated) and let the sentence run into the existing next sentence about Case 3 instead. No renumbering needed — Brief Report's citation of Okazaki (its ref 10, for the gastric-volvulus/pancreatic-tail sentence) was already correct and untouched.

Word counts after these fixes: Original Article 2621/3000; Brief Report 1275/1500.

## Journal-abbreviation audit (21 Sep 2026)
Checked all 20 (Original Article) / 12 (Brief Report) journal-abbreviation forms against actual PubMed records (not just NLM Catalog convention guessing). One confirmed error, fixed in both documents:
- **Colombo et al. 2019** ("Torsion of wandering spleen involving the pancreatic tail"): PubMed's actual abbreviation is **"Ann Med Surg (Lond)"**, not the bare "Ann Med Surg" that was used — "Annals of Medicine and Surgery" needs the "(Lond)" disambiguator per NLM. Fixed in both `EJP original article.docx` (ref 7) and `EJP brief report.docx` (ref 11).

Also independently confirmed via PubMed (previously unverified): Al Blooshi et al. 2025 (BMC Pediatr 25:631 — correct as written) and Gashaw Wubie et al. 2025 (J Med Case Rep 19:41 — correct as written). All other abbreviations (J Laparoendosc Adv Surg Tech A, Front Pediatr, J Pediatr Surg, Pediatr Radiol, Pediatr Emerg Care, Isr Med Assoc J, Pediatr Surg Int, J Minim Access Surg, Asian J Endosc Surg, J Med Case Rep, J Radiol Case Rep, BMC Pediatr) were confirmed correct via PubMed listings already surfaced during the citation-content audit, or independently searched. **The full reference list has now been verified end-to-end: content-accuracy (citation-correspondence audit) and bibliographic-format (this abbreviation audit) both checked against primary sources, not just titles.**

## Title — final wording (21 Sep 2026)
Author asked whether the comma before "and a reinforced two-pole splenopexy" could be dropped, or whether a better title existed. Analysis: unlike the earlier round (where "and" joined two parallel objects of "determines" and the comma was safely droppable), in this title "and" sits right after "rather than technique" — dropping the comma would make "rather than technique and a reinforced two-pole splenopexy" read as a single compound object of "rather than," reversing the meaning (implying the technique is also being contrasted away, not separately introduced). So the comma could not simply be removed.

**Final title (both EJP versions): "Wandering spleen in children: splenic loss is associated with delay rather than technique, and a reinforced two-pole splenopexy is described."** Added "is described" so the second half is a full independent clause, making the comma a standard clause-joining comma rather than a fragile disambiguator, while keeping the technique named per the author's original requirement. Applied to both `EJP original article.docx` and `EJP brief report.docx`, rebuilt, schema-validated, committed.

## Final pre-submission check (21 Sep 2026)
At the author's request, Claude did a full read-through of both final EJP files plus a programmatic re-audit of citations, word counts and orphan/out-of-range references. Found and fixed 4 new issues (all applied to both documents where relevant, rebuilt, schema-validated, re-audited clean — 0 missing/out-of-range refs in either document):

1. **Introduction citation mismatch (both versions).** "...the literature consists almost entirely of single case reports and series of two or three patients" cited ref3/Fiquet-Francois — but ref3 is the multicentre study (already correctly used elsewhere as the "larger" counter-example), not a small series. Removed ref3 from this citation: Original Article `[3, 4]` → `[4]`; Brief Report `[1, 3]` → `[1, 12]` (swapped in Al Blooshi 2025, whose own paper is literally "three cases," a genuine fit).
2. **Brief Report: ref9 (Zivkovic, button-and-hole technique) was an orphan** — never cited in the text (the Original Article's equivalent sentence citing it had been cut when the Brief Report was condensed, but the reference-list entry was left behind). Fixed by restoring the equivalent sentence in the Discussion's technique paragraph: "A comparable rationale underlies the sutureless button-and-hole method, which likewise avoids reliance on the capsule alone [9]" (mirrors the Original Article's already-verified wording). The ref3 freed up by fix 1 was given a legitimate home instead, at the Discussion's "supports operating at the time of diagnosis... [3]" sentence — mirroring exactly how ref3 is used in the Original Article's parallel sentence. The now-redundant "[12]" citation on the unrelated "untested" sentence at the end of the Brief Report's Limitations paragraph was removed (the Original Article's parallel sentence was never cited either).
3. **Brief Report Table 1, Case 3 row said "three episodes managed conservatively"; corrected to "two episodes."** Case 3 had 4 total encounters: 2 conservative (encounters 1–2), then her *first splenic operation* (partial splenectomy + capsular splenopexy, encounter 3 — a surgical encounter, not conservative), then total splenectomy (encounter 4) a month later. Only 2 of the 4 were conservative management.
4. **Original Article "What is New" box, 2nd bullet, reworded for clarity.** Old wording ("...two-pole fixation... salvaged a spleen after a capsular splenopexy had failed") could be misread as the same spleen/patient being rescued after a failed capsular splenopexy. In fact the two-pole technique (Case 4) and the failed capsular splenopexy (Case 3) are two different patients — Case 4 never had a prior capsular splenopexy. New wording: "...full-thickness two-pole fixation buttressed with haemostatic gauze preserved the spleen in a separate patient, developed in response to an earlier capsular-splenopexy failure."

Word counts after these fixes: Original Article 2624/3000; Brief Report 1297/1500 — both still comfortably under EJP's limits. No placeholder/TODO/highlight remnants found in either final docx.

## English-language polish pass (21 Sep 2026)
Author asked for a pure language polish of both EJP documents, under explicit constraints: no change to any number, abbreviation or reference number; no new data or references; do not strengthen "associated with" into causal language; do not alter statistical wording; remove repetition and colloquialisms; concise standard medical-journal English; remove AI-sounding phrasing. Re-verified after: citation audit clean (no orphan/out-of-range refs in either document), word counts essentially unchanged (Original Article 2614/3000, Brief Report 1296/1500).

Changes applied — wording only, no facts, numbers, hedge strength or reference numbers altered:
- **Original Article**, 6 edits: tightened an unparallel Abstract sentence (mixed "the family declined" / "one had been treated" subjects) into a parallel "in three, ...; in one, ..." construction; removed a throat-clearing Discussion opener ("The central observation of this series is that..." → states the finding directly); simplified a wordplay sentence ("That resolution is not evidence of a resolved problem" → "This does not indicate that the underlying problem has resolved"); de-stacked a sentence that hedged the same idea three times ("is thought to require... may mark..." → single "we hypothesize that" framing carried through once, same overall epistemic status); replaced a colloquial "is worth seeking specifically" with "should be sought specifically"; removed a redundant "both because... because..." construction.
- **Brief Report**, 1 edit: "a period of functional asplenia of sufficient duration" → "a sufficiently long period of functional asplenia" (redundant period/duration doubling).

No other passages needed changing — the text had already been through several precision-focused editing rounds and had little remaining redundancy or informal phrasing.

## EJP outcome and pivot to case series + systematic review (Sep 2026)
- **EJP desk-rejected** the Original Article (no reviewer comments). Next-journal shortlist discussed: Pediatric Surgery International first choice for a case series.
- **Decided: upgrade to "institutional case series + systematic review with individual patient data (IPD)"**, extracting interval to surgery and splenic preservation for every published paediatric case. Chinese-language reports to be included (NLM format).
- Author placed 53 hand-collected PDFs on origin/main (`wandering spleen literature.zip`). All 53 read; pilot extraction in `literature-pilot-extraction-53-papers.csv` (91 rows, status column flags excluded/aggregate/duplicate rows).

### Pilot findings (hand-curated sample — not a systematic search)
- 45 papers contribute IPD on **76 unique children**; 16 lost the spleen (21%). Interval reported in 54/76; the 18 with no interval reported were all preserved (reporting bias).
- Loss by interval: <1 wk 3/17; **1 wk–1 mo 9/17**; 1–12 mo 3/14; **>12 mo 0/6**; asymptomatic 1/4. Non-monotonic: acute (index-episode) delay shows a signal, long chronic histories mostly preserved — own Cases 1–3 (16–62 mo, lost) run against the literature pattern, so the primary exposure should be redefined (index-episode duration primary; total history, missed diagnosis, known-but-deferred secondary).
- Known-but-deferred literature cases: 5; 3 torsed while awaiting surgery (35-3, 52-1, 53-2), all salvaged — supports "operate at diagnosis" via a torsion-while-waiting outcome.
- Outcome must separate non-viable spleen at operation from viable spleens removed for technical reasons (Wang 2022 case 3, 16-cm spleen) and post-splenopexy losses (Cohen 2018 case 1; Fiquet-Francois 3/5 mesh ischaemia).
- GV/GOO-associated subgroup: 19 cases, 18 preserved — analyse separately.
- **Prior SR exists: Ganarin 2021 (JLAST, PROSPERO CRD42018089971, 197 children, English only, 1990–2018) — no delay/interval analysis.** Novelty = time exposure with IPD, 2019–2026 update, Chinese literature.
- Duplicates: 13 and 19 within 22; 31-1 = 18-1; 35-4/5 = 04-1/4; 39-1 = 25. Paper 03 (李索林) is 中华医学杂志 **2000**;80(1):37 despite "1999" filename.
- Still needed: documented database search (PubMed, Embase, WoS, Scopus, CNKI, Wanfang, CBM), second reviewer, protocol registration (PROSPERO/OSF, declaring the pilot), IPD requests to authors of aggregate-only series.

### Decisions for the SR manuscript (28 Sep 2026)
- **Primary exposure: duration of the index symptomatic episode** (onset of the current continuous symptoms → operation, in days). Primary analysis on the continuous scale (log-days) to avoid choosing cut-points after seeing the pilot; categories secondary. The pilot informed this hypothesis and must be declared as such in the protocol.
- **Secondary exposures:** total history from first attributable symptom; missed diagnosis (prior contact/imaging without diagnosis); known diagnosis with surgery deferred (includes own Cases 1–3).
- **Primary outcome:** spleen non-viable at operation (total or partial splenectomy for infarction). Reported separately: viable spleen removed for technical reasons, post-splenopexy loss, torsion while awaiting surgery.
- **Technique NOT in the title.** Working title: "Time to surgery and splenic salvage in children with wandering spleen: a single-centre case series and systematic review of individual patient data". The reinforced two-pole technique stays in the Discussion (enlarged-spleen fixation context) and the schematic figure, described as n=1.
- Independent reviewers: **Jun Shu** (reviewer 1) and **Fei Peng** (reviewer 2), both confirmed by the author 28 Sep 2026; adjudicator Jun Yang; statistical analysis **Ji Wang**. **Embase and Scopus both accessible** — kept in the search plan.

### Candidate journals for the SR + case-series paper (discussed 28 Sep 2026)

ORCID for the other 4 team members listed in the record is optional/recommended, not mandatory — can be left blank if unavailable. Protocol document updated with this distinction as an italic note above the review-team table; the 5 ORCID cells remain yellow-highlighted pending whatever each author supplies.

- **PROSPERO amendment — timing decided (28 Sep 2026): defer, submit once after formal searches are complete**, rather than amending now for v1.1 alone. Rationale discussed with the author: v1.0 was registered before formal extraction/screening began, which is within PROSPERO's normal allowance (registration must precede data extraction, not every methodological detail); amending once, after the formal searches are actually run, avoids a multi-amendment trail and lets the single update carry the as-run search strategy together with the search log (dates, hits, indexes/years for WoS, CNKI 游离脾 check, Google Scholar number browsed) rather than a strategy that might still change before the searches are executed. Amendment note to use at that point: "Search strategies (Appendix A) finalised after review by a medical librarian and confirmed by the formal searches run on [dates]; eligibility criteria, exposures, outcomes and analysis unchanged."
- **PROSPERO amendment procedure (Jun Shu, as record owner, to action when the formal searches are done):** upload the final protocol PDF to CRD420261520329. Procedure (per web search of third-party PROSPERO-guide sites only — crd.york.ac.uk itself is blocked by the container's proxy, so **Jun Shu should verify the exact button/menu wording on the live site**): log into the My PROSPERO dashboard with the account that owns CRD420261520329, open the record, and look for an "Amend record" option. PROSPERO is reported to distinguish a "Minor amendment" (a few fields only) from a "Major revision" (full record access) — changing review methods/search strategy is reported to require the Major revision route. Each amendment is reported to require a short stated reason for the change and to be logged in a dated, publicly visible audit trail (prior versions stay accessible). If no self-service amendment option is found on the record, the PROSPERO help/contact page is the fallback.

- **Embase Emtree check done (29 Sep 2026; four screenshots saved as `search/embase/emtree_*_2026-09-29.*`):**
  - **wandering spleen = an Emtree preferred term.** Tree positions: diseases > physical disease > physical disease by etiology and pathogenesis > prolapse > visceral prolapse > wandering spleen; physical disease by anatomical structure > digestive system disease > visceral prolapse > wandering spleen; and a third position under hematologic disease > lymphatic system disease > spleen disease (last node hidden by a browser popup in the screenshot). "Extend search using: /exp"; Emtree showed "Show 557 results" (a live count on the day, not the formal record). **Synonyms (9):** floating spleen, movable spleen, ptotic spleen, spleen ptosis, splenic ptosis, splenoptosis, visceroptosed spleen, visceroptotic spleen, wandering spleen. (Four of these — ptotic spleen, spleen ptosis [partly by `ptos*` in #5], visceroptosed spleen, visceroptotic spleen — are not in the free-text lines #2/#5; records indexed with the preferred term are still reached through #1, so no change to the strings was made, to keep the databases consistent.)
  - **spleen torsion** and **splenic torsion**: both shown as **"candidate terms"** ("It will be evaluated for possible inclusion in Emtree") — **not** preferred terms, no tree position, no synonyms. Live counts 97 and 33. Per protocol A2 they are **not added** to #1; they stay covered by the free-text lines #3 and #5.
  - **splenopexy = an Emtree preferred term, added to Emtree in 2025** (tree: procedures, parameters and devices > procedures > medical procedures > surgery > abdominal surgery > spleen surgery > splenopexy; also surgical technique > surgical fixation > splenopexy). Synonyms (3): spleen fixation, splenic fixation, splenopexy. Live count only 30 (recent term, so older records carry it rarely). **Per protocol A2 rule** ("any that is a separate preferred term is added to #1 with OR and /exp") **#1 becomes `'wandering spleen'/exp OR 'splenopexy'/exp`.** Must be written into the A2 note as run.

**Deduplication estimate (4 Oct 2026; script `tools/dedup_estimate.py`, outputs in `search/dedup_estimate/`: summary, one row per record with cluster id, ambiguous pairs).** An estimate to cross-check the formal EndNote deduplication, **not** a replacement for it (the protocol specifies EndNote). Result: **estimated 2,695 unique records, 2,211 duplicates.** By tier (applied in order): T1 identical DOI 1,293; T2 identical title + same first author + year ±1 823; T2b cross-script English title (CBM parallel title) 3; T3 similar title (ratio ≥0.90) + same first author + year ±1 92. Cumulative unique: DOI only 3,613; DOI + exact title/author/year 2,790; all tiers 2,695. Not merged (listed for manual review): 2,269 identical-title pairs — 2,224 have different authors (generic titles such as "Wandering spleen." used by many different papers, correctly kept apart), **33 with a missing author and 12 with the same author but years >1 apart**, which are the ones worth a manual look. Within-source duplicates: PubMed 0, WoS 2, Scopus 9, CNKI 7, Wanfang 11, CBM 3. Spot checks: DOI merges with dissimilar titles (27) are Chinese records sharing a DOI with their English-titled Scopus/PubMed entries (genuine cross-script duplicates); fuzzy merges inspected (bracketed/translated titles, "β" vs "beta", trailing "[6]") looked correct; the 141 short-title merges all rely on same first author + year ±1. **Overlap:** unique records found by 1 source 1,193; by 2 sources 836; by 3 sources 656; by 4 sources 9; by 5 sources 1. Found only by the three English sources 1,881, only by the three Chinese sources 800, by both groups 14 (cross-script matching is mostly by DOI, so true overlap may be a little higher). Per-source unique contribution: PubMed 208, WoS 88, Scopus 398, CNKI 223, Wanfang 125, CBM 151. **If EndNote ends far from 2,695 (say >150 away), look at the records CSV to see which tier explains the gap** — expected PRISMA numbers if it agrees: identified 4,906, duplicates removed 2,211, records to screen 2,695.

**[Numbers in this paragraph are SUPERSEDED by the update paragraph below: final 2,550 unique records.] Scripted deduplication done (4 Oct 2026; script `tools/dedup.py`, outputs in `search/dedup/`) — the author asked Claude to do it because EndNote cannot run in the cloud container; supersedes the earlier estimate in `search/dedup_estimate/` (2,695), which lacked the author-splitting fix and tiers T4/T5.** **4,906 records identified → 2,555 unique after removing 2,351 duplicates.** By tier (applied in order): T1 DOI 1,293; T2 identical title + same first author + year ±1, 878; T2b cross-script English title 3; T3 similar title (≥0.90) + same author + year ±1, 94; T4 same author + year + volume + first page, 58; T5 identical title + same volume + compatible pages, 22; manual merges 3 (CNKI/Wanfang pairs listed in the script's DECISIONS: 护士进修杂志 超声检查… p1160, 侯利民 脾免疫耐受 2004, 冯平勇 游走脾并脾蒂扭转二例 2005). **Safeguards:** hard blocks stop merging when both volumes differ and first pages are >10 apart, or when two records of the same database have incompatible pages or different first authors (keeps e.g. Fakler 2004 ×2, Choh 2008 ×2, 邱江涛 2008 ×2 apart as separate publications); merges are cluster-aware and ordered so records with matching pages are joined first. 2,111 identical-title pairs with different first authors were left apart (generic titles like "Wandering spleen."); 52 further pairs were reviewed one by one and kept apart (different journals/volumes/pages/years, 10 bee-keeping patents from Wanfang, animal studies) — all in `residual_pairs_for_review.csv` with a decision column. Per source (identified / duplicates within the source / only in this source): PubMed 1263/0/164; WoS 750/1/81; Scopus 1563/7/354; CNKI 486/4/144; Wanfang 423/5/107; CBM 421/1/121. Final file `records_deduplicated.ris` (2,555 records, UTF-8, one per cluster, ID WS0001…; master record = the one with abstract, then DOI, then source priority PubMed > Scopus > WoS > Wanfang > CNKI > CBM; missing fields filled from duplicates; `N1` lists every source and source ID, and alternative titles). 1,922 have an abstract, 1,600 a DOI; 720 are Chinese-language records. `dedup_map_all_records.csv` maps every original record to its final ID (audit trail); `cluster_conflicts_for_review.csv` lists 65 merged clusters whose members differ in DOI/volume/page — mostly benign (page formats, Spanish/English editions with different DOIs, WoS DOI typos), not individually re-checked. **Not verified:** that EndNote agrees; true duplicates the rules cannot see (e.g. a Chinese record vs an English record without DOI or parallel title) will survive and should be marked in Rayyan. Protocol Appendix B "Records after deduplication" row filled (rule-based script, 2,555, EndNote check pending); the PROSPERO amendment draft states the numbers with a bracket for the EndNote check. **The protocol's Section 5 still says "deduplicated in EndNote" — reword once the EndNote check is done.**

Dataset facts quoted in the guide: 2,555 records, 633 without abstract, 721 Chinese, types JOUR 2,437 / PAT 50 / GEN 21 / CHAP 20 / CONF 14 / THES 10 / NEWS 3; keyword counts are rough workload estimates and must not be used to auto-exclude. **Six decisions left to the author (section 8 of the guide):** Chinese full conference papers, theses, mixed adult/child series, keeping the `review-for-refs` tag, reporting screening agreement, re-screening the 53 pilot reports (protocol says yes). **Word version made 4 Oct 2026** (`Screening guide - title and abstract (draft).docx`, generated from the .md by `tools/md_to_docx.js`: 15 headings, 2 tables, lists; schema-validated; **not visually rendered** because LibreOffice cannot load .docx in the container; every line of the .md was checked to be present in the .docx text). **Rayyan labels and exclusion reasons — correction (5 Oct 2026):** Claude had told the author to "build" E1–E6 and the two labels as a setup step; per a web search of Rayyan's help centre (search-result text only — help.rayyan.ai is blocked by the container proxy, the article itself was not read) **there is no pre-configuration: reasons and labels are created on the fly** (open a record → decision bar → Reason or Label → type a new name → tick → Apply; it is then saved in the review's list), and only exclusion reasons appear in the PRISMA diagram. Two things flagged to the author and written into the screening guide (.md + regenerated .docx): (1) **Rayyan's default highlight keywords are RCT-oriented and misleading here** — Keywords for exclude lists `case report` (10 hits in the 50), `cells`, `murine`, `animals`, `transgenic`, `this review`, `case reports`, `literature review`, `rat`, `fish`; Keywords for include lists CCT, RCT, trial, placebo, randomly, crossover, randomised, assigned to; the guide tells both screeners to delete `case report`, `case reports`, `literature review`, `this review` etc. and use the guide's own lists (whether highlights are per user or shared is unverified); (2) **AI features (AI Reviewer, Analyze, ResearchPilot chat) must not be used for decisions** — protocol v1.2 does not mention AI or automation tools (checked by grep of `tools/build_protocol.js`: no match); using them would need a protocol change and a Methods statement. **Is Rayyan blind mode "required"? (author's question, 5 Oct 2026):** checked the protocol text in `tools/build_protocol.js`: Section 14 says two reviewers **independently** screen titles/abstracts then full texts, disagreements by discussion or a third reviewer (JY); it does **not** mention Rayyan's blind mode or "double blind". **Rayyan member removal — Revoke vs Delete (author's screenshot, browser-translated page again, 5 Oct 2026):** the row menu of the old reviewer account offers "撤销会员资格" (Revoke member) and "删除成员" (Delete member). Per Rayyan help-centre search results (summary only; page not read): **Revoke** removes access but keeps the member's decisions, labels and notes visible (reported as Essential-and-higher plans); **Delete** removes access and hides the member's contributions (all plans); in both cases the data are preserved and re-inviting the same email brings them back. Advice: use **Delete** for the shared-email account (it made no decisions; its contributions must not appear in the calibration), then invite Fei Peng's own address. Not re-inviting the old address. **Rayyan default exclusion reasons seen; "reason optional?" answered (5 Oct 2026, author's screenshots of the calibration Screening tab, English interface).** (1) The "Exclude with Reasons" dialog already lists Rayyan's **built-in default reasons**: background article, foreign language, wrong drug, wrong outcome, wrong population, wrong publication type, wrong study design (list cut off in the image — more may follow), each with a "Click to add hotkey" button, and an "Add exclusion reasons" box + Apply at the bottom. **Claude's earlier note that reasons are "created on the fly, no pre-configuration" was incomplete:** our E1–E6 do have to be typed in by the first reviewer, but the defaults are pre-populated and must **not** be used (they are intervention-review categories and do not map to E1–E6; mixing them breaks comparison and the PRISMA reasons). Guide section 3 (.md and regenerated .docx) now says so. Not verified: whether the defaults can be deleted, and how exactly a new reason is confirmed in the box (Enter vs Apply) — check in the live interface. The same screenshot shows the author's own decisions on records 01–04 (three Exclude, one Maybe) — **which reasons were attached to them was not visible; the author should check they are not default reasons** (re-select E1–E6 if so; decisions themselves are not discussed, to keep calibration independent). (2) Author asked whether a reason can be skipped: in the same screenshot Exclude and Reason are separate buttons, so Exclude alone probably works (unverified — the help page cannot be read); the protocol and PRISMA 2020 require reasons only at full-text stage, and guide line 161 already calls title/abstract reasons optional while section 3 says "must" — an inconsistency in the guide. **Recommendation given: keep selecting reasons during the 50-record calibration (they show where the guide is unclear; both reviewers must do the same); decide after calibration, together with the six items in guide section 8, whether to make reasons optional at title/abstract stage for the 2,548 screening (would need guide line 32 changed).** No guide change made on this point yet.

**Calibration results — exports received and analysed (7 Oct 2026).** The author found Rayyan's own export: **Review data → "···" → Review settings panel → Export** (dialog: All references (50), CSV, ticked abstracts/decisions/exclusion reasons/labels/user notes) and uploaded two files: `articles.csv` (50 rows; the `notes` field carries `RAYYAN-INCLUSION: {"俊"=>…, "Fei"=>…}` per-reviewer decisions and one merged `RAYYAN-EXCLUSION-REASONS` string) and `customizations_log.csv` (event log: UTC timestamp, Rayyan user id, **e-mail**, article id, key, value). **The raw uploads are NOT stored in the repo (they contain reviewer e-mail addresses).** Saved instead: `search/screening/calibration_results_filled.csv` (one row per record: WS id, Rayyan id, year, title, R1/R2 decision and reason, has_abstract, language), `search/screening/calibration_decisions_log_deidentified.csv` (all events, reviewers labelled R1_JunShu/R2_FeiPeng, no e-mail or user id) and `tools/calib_analysis.py` (rerun: `python3 -I tools/calib_analysis.py articles.csv log.csv search/screening/calibration_set_50_ids.csv <out_results> <out_log>`). The empty `calibration_results_template.csv` was deleted (superseded). **Coding verified:** log value 1 = Include, 0 = Maybe, −1 = Exclude, latest event per user/record wins; the result matches `RAYYAN-INCLUSION` in articles.csv for all 50 × 2 decisions (0 mismatches); all 50 records map one-to-one to WS ids by title+year. **Results:** R1 (Jun Shu) Exclude 22 / Maybe 21 / Include 7; R2 (Fei Peng) Exclude 26 / Maybe 18 / Include 6. **Agreement: 45/50 = 90.0%, Cohen's kappa 0.834 (3 categories); 46/50 = 92.0%, kappa 0.841 for advance-to-full-text (Include or Maybe) vs Exclude** (rough 95% CI for the 3-category kappa ≈ 0.70–0.97 by the simple formula, n = 50). Contingency (R1 rows × R2 cols, Include/Maybe/Exclude): Include [6,1,0]; Maybe [0,17,4]; Exclude [0,0,22]. **All 5 disagreements are in the 11 records WITHOUT an abstract (0 of 39 records with an abstract disagree):** WS1142 (R1 Include, R2 Maybe: "[Torsion of a floating spleen in a 13-year-old girl]" 1967); WS1151, WS1191, WS1241, WS2143 (R1 Maybe, R2 Exclude: 1966 intrapulmonary splenopexy in portal hypertension; 1957 genital tumours & floating spleens; 1946 leukaemic spleen with twisted pedicle; 放射学诊断拾零 2000). **R2's four Excludes on those records carry NO exclusion reason** (no reason event in the log) although guide section 3 says Exclude must have a fixed reason and 4.3 says title-only records are Maybe unless clearly irrelevant — i.e. R2 excluded on title judgement where no E1–E6 applies. **Exclusion reasons were identical in all 22 both-Exclude records** (E3 ×10, E2 ×7, E1 ×3, E4 ×2); the "two applicable reasons" worry noted earlier produced no disagreement (record 04 carries E3 for both) — latent gap only. No default Rayyan reasons were used by either reviewer; R1 first typed a non-standard reason "adults only" (record 04) and deleted it within ~3 min after creating `E3 adults only`; R1 changed record 05's reason from E3 to E2 (decision unchanged). **Timing (UTC; China time = +8):** R1 first decision 12:57:32 → last first-decision 13:45:56 on 5 Oct = 47.9 min for 50 records (median gap 19 s, 3 gaps >120 s); R2 13:49:21 → 13:59:08 = 9.8 min (median gap 12 s, no gap >120 s). These are spans between decisions, not including time reading the first record. **Independence note:** R2 started after R1 finished; R1 re-entered one decision (record #716293586: set Include 13:35:38, deleted 13:45:56, set Include again 14:04:10) after R2's last decision (13:59:08) — final value equals R1's original, so no change of substance, but state it if asked. The Show-clicked question is not answerable from the log (still asked of the author). **Candidate guide changes (none applied yet; need the author/Fei Peng/Jun Yang):** (a) title-only (no abstract) rule — spell out that Exclude needs a fixed reason, otherwise Maybe, with the old/adult-looking-title examples from this set; (b) Include vs Maybe on title alone (WS1142) — both advance to full text, so no practical effect, but define it; (c) priority when two exclusion reasons apply (latent); (d) reason mandatory or optional at title/abstract stage (links to guide line 32 vs line 161 inconsistency). Rough workload from calibration rates: 2,548 records × 12–19 s ≈ 8.5–13.5 h per screener (extrapolation from n = 50; the sample is random, so reasonable but crude).

**Screening guide revised after the calibration (7 Oct 2026; author agreed to all four recommendations, "同意，按你的建议改").** Changes in `Screening guide - title and abstract (draft).md` (source) and regenerated `.docx` (schema-validated; 202 text segments of the .md all found in the .docx; not visually rendered): (1) Exclude must name an applicable E1–E6, otherwise choose Maybe (section 3 and the opening of section 4); (2) no-abstract records use only Maybe or Exclude, never Include, and Exclude needs a clear E-reason (4.3 row; old-looking/adult-looking/other-specialty titles that contain wandering/floating/torsion words and have no clear reason → Maybe); (3) reasons stay mandatory at title/abstract stage (guide line 32 kept; the earlier line-32-vs-161 inconsistency is resolved in favour of "mandatory" — section 9 still says "可选按理由分类" for the PRISMA count of title/abstract exclusions, which only concerns reporting, not the reviewers' task); (4) if two reasons apply any applicable one is fine (section 5; calibration examples WS0250 accessory spleen in adults → both E3, WS0303 accessory-spleen torsion in a child → both E2); plus (5) check at the start of every session that author/journal are hidden, record and tell the others if shown (section 3); (6) export method and "do not click Detect duplicates / Auto resolve" added to section 3; (7) new **section 11** records the calibration results, the revision table, what was not changed (section 8 items 1–4 and 6 still open; two screeners kept), and the limitation (n = 50; only 11 no-abstract records). **Approval status: Jun Shu agreed; Fei Peng and Jun Yang have not yet confirmed the changes** (the guide header says items need all three). **Before the formal screening starts (2,548-record project, Blind ON, nothing screened):** Fei Peng/Jun Yang confirm the guide; the first reviewer creates `E1 non-human`…`E6 other` and the two labels in the formal project (they are per review); both clean the Rayyan highlight keyword lists there as in the calibration project (per-user vs shared unverified); both confirm author/journal hidden; decide Jun Yang's Rayyan access (Reviewer/Viewer roles; whether a Viewer can resolve conflicts unverified; fallback = resolve outside Rayyan and log the adjudication); decide the six items in guide section 8.

**Correction on Rayyan's highlight keywords (10 Oct 2026, computed from the repo and the author's 7 Oct screenshots).** Rayyan highlights/counts whole words only: for 19 English keywords the counts shown by Rayyan (torsion 891, wandering 871, splenopexy 218, volvulus 215, child 103, pediatric 92, accessory 179, dogs 78, …) are reproduced exactly by a case-insensitive whole-word match on title + abstract (17 exact, 2 off by one) — so `child` does not highlight "children". **Chinese keywords are under-counted heavily:** 游走脾 shows 20 in Rayyan but 162 of the 2,548 records contain it in title or abstract; 患儿 18 vs 54; 脾蒂扭转 6 vs 68; 儿童 0 vs 42; 小儿 0 vs 18; 副脾 4 vs 39; 小鼠 2 vs 19. Counting only occurrences whose both neighbours are non-letter characters (punctuation/space/boundary) reproduces Rayyan's Chinese numbers closely (游走脾 19, 患儿 18, 脾蒂扭转 5, 脾下垂 4, 漂浮脾 1, 儿童 0, 小儿 0, 副脾 4, 小鼠 1 against Rayyan 20, 18, 6, 4, 1, 0, 0, 4, 2) — an inference from the counts, not from Rayyan documentation. **This corrects the 7 Oct statement** that the Chinese 0s in the calibration set were only due to the 9 Chinese records containing no keyword: Chinese hits exist but cover only a small part of the records that contain the word, so a Chinese record without highlight means nothing. Screening decisions are unaffected (the guide already says highlights are an aid). Creating E5 is a real Exclude decision by the creator; Fei Peng must screen the same record independently (with WS0091, WS0157, WS0095, WS0689).
