**E5 search failed a third time; the screenshot shows the Fei Peng account already has formal-screening decisions (11 Oct 2026; author's message "蜂箱找不到" with a screenshot).** Screenshot facts (new.rayyan.ai, review 2349772, Screening tab, English interface): Blind Mode ON; the search box holds 蜂箱 and the filter bar reads "All articles AND Search ×", but the header still says "Showing 2,548 articles" and the list is in the original import order (list position 01 = the first record, Rayyan id 716321890, WS0001), so the Chinese query had no effect — third failure after 7 Oct and 10 Oct; Claude's estimate of 20–60 hits on 11 Oct was wrong. Inference, not verified: Rayyan's search ignores non-Latin characters. No English-token workaround exists: of the 25 Latin/numeric tokens in the patents' titles and abstracts only "4.15" is absent from every other record. **The decision chips in the list and on the open record read "Fei"; in blind mode a member sees only their own decisions, so this is the Fei Peng account, and it carries decisions on at least the first nine list records** (individual decisions deliberately not transcribed or commented on). So formal screening has started; the date of Fei Peng's first decision is not known (the Rayyan export log gives it after unblinding), and who operated the account was asked, not yet answered. The screenshot, which Claude had asked for, also exposed one reviewer's decisions on ten records; future screenshots for Claude should show only the search box, header and buttons, not the decision chips. **Consequences for the PROSPERO amendment (ON HOLD; a HOLD notice was put at the top of `PROSPERO amendment note - final text.md`):** protocol v1.3 (stage table "at the time of amendment", Section 14) and the amendment note (section 1 "Amended before formal screening started", section 5 "Formal screening: No / No") say formal screening has not started. That is no longer true, and was already imprecise on 7 Oct: the author had recorded four real Exclude decisions in the formal project (WS0091, WS0157, WS0095, WS0689) to create the reasons E1–E4, which Claude treated as set-up rather than as the start of screening. To do once the dates are confirmed: set Formal screening to Started / Not completed; state the date of the first reviewer's four decisions (7 Oct) and of the second reviewer's first decision; say that protocol v1.3's screening procedure was finalised on 7 Oct before the second reviewer's decisions; bump the protocol to v1.4 and rewrite the amendment note. Also open: whether any amendment was already submitted (recorded as not submitted on 10 Oct). **Decision on E5:** not created; not needed to start screening; whoever first meets a patent, newspaper item or abstract-only conference record creates `E5 not a formal article` and the other selects it from the list. In import order such records begin after about position 1,600 (English conference abstracts WS1632 and WS1696 need reading; Chinese conference papers from WS1858; newspapers WS1996–WS1998, which are also E1; patents WS2375–WS2426). An optional English control search (`splenopexy`: the count should fall from 2,548) was suggested to close manual item V7. Guide and manual not changed.

**Author's answers and the PROSPERO material corrected — protocol v1.4 (11 Oct 2026).** The author answered two of the three questions: **Fei Peng herself operated the account** in the 11 Oct screenshot, and **the PROSPERO amendment had not been submitted**. The date of Fei Peng's first formal decision was not given (the Rayyan decision log will show it after unblinding), so the texts say "between 7 and 11 October 2026" (7 Oct: her formal-project screen showed no decisions; 11 Oct: decisions on at least the first nine records). **Changes (tools/build_protocol.js, protocol .docx rebuilt, schema-validated, 411 paragraphs, 0 highlights; diff against the committed v1.3 shows only these):** cover "Version 1.4, 11 October 2026"; version history: v1.3 no longer says "before formal screening started" and a v1.4 clause explains the correction; stage table "at the time of amendment (version 1.4, 11 October 2026)" now has Formal screening Started = Yes, Completed = No; new Section 14 paragraph "Status of formal screening (added in version 1.4)" (according to the project log the first reviewer recorded four formal decisions on 7 Oct to create four of the six exclusion reasons; the second reviewer screens those four records independently; second reviewer's first decisions between 7 and 11 Oct; the written screening rules had been confirmed by both reviewers and the adjudicator on 7 Oct, before the second reviewer's first decision; exact dates and per-reviewer counts to be taken from the Rayyan log after the stage and reported). `PROSPERO amendment note - final text.md`: HOLD notice replaced by a revision note; status line; attach v1.4; section 1 reason ("Formal screening had already started when this amendment was made, so the stage of review is updated") and its short fallback; section 2 and 7 version references; section 3 renamed and given a "Timing of this amendment" paragraph; section 4 pointer; section 5 table ticked Yes / No and explained. Eligibility, exposures, outcomes, analysis and dates unchanged. The guide and manual still cite "protocol v1.3" for the blind-mode and AI statements, which remain true in v1.4 (v1.4 changes only the stage-of-review statements); update those references at the next guide revision, no resend needed. **Blinding event to report (Methods/Limitations):** on 11 Oct 2026 the first reviewer (the author) saw a screenshot, requested by Claude, that showed the second reviewer's decisions on the first records of the list (positions 01–09 carried decisions; WS0001–WS0010 in import order). Whether the author had already screened those records is not known. Advice given: if not yet screened, decide from the title and abstract alone without using the screenshot, and report the exposure in the Methods; do not forward screenshots with decision chips between reviewers before both finish the stage. Still open: the amendment itself (Jun Shu: export the v1.4 PDF, tick Formal screening Started / Not completed, paste sections 1–4 of the note, send screenshots of the submission).

**11 Oct 2026 (later): the author showed WS1875 and asked "这个是E5吗？" (screenshot cropped to the right pane, no decision chips).** Rayyan id 716323764 = 716321889 + 1875 (fourth confirmation of the id formula: WS0001, WS0091, WS1875, WS1890). WS1875 = "中蜂桶的栅档与巢虫之关系", TY JOUR, a bee-keeping article from CNKI whose 巢脾 (honeycomb) most likely triggered the Chinese term 脾固定 (cause not verified). It is a formal journal article, so **not E5** (E5 is a record type: patent, newspaper, product description, abstract-only conference record). The natural reason is E2 "not wandering spleen" (as for the pacemaker paper in the calibration list); E6 would need a note. The guide does not name unrelated non-medical records — candidate row for the next guide revision (4.3: unrelated non-medical article, e.g. bee-keeping → E2); title/abstract reasons do not change who advances. Warned the author not to create E5 on this record (it would attach a wrong reason). Patent recognition cues from `records_deduplicated.ris`: abstract starts 本实用新型 (25 of 50), 本发明 (18) or 一种… (the rest); title starts 一种 in only 24 of 50; none of the 50 patents has an abstract starting with <正> (that CNKI marker is on 166 records: 156 journal articles, 10 conference records). Rayyan ids of the patent block by the formula: 716324264–716324315, except 716324304 (WS2415) and 716324306 (WS2417), which are GEN records; the formula is verified only up to WS1890, so it is a pre-check, not proof. The other bee-keeping journal articles (not E5): WS1909, WS1928, WS2006, WS2106, WS2269, WS2343, WS2345, WS2346, WS2366. Asked the author how WS1875 was reached (search term or scrolling) to learn whether single-character Chinese searches work (manual item V7). E5 still not created.

**11 Oct 2026 (later still): the author searched Rayyan for the id 716324264 and found the patent WS2375 (screenshot cropped to the search box, one record and the decision bar, no decision chips; question "这个是吗").** The list showed exactly one record (position 01) while the header still read "Showing 2,548 articles", so **the header count does not follow the search — judge a search by the list, not by the header.** Claude's 11 Oct rule "header still ~2,5xx = the search is not narrowing" was wrong as an indicator (the 蜂箱 search did leave the list itself unfiltered, so the failure of Chinese-text queries stands, but the header was no evidence either way). **Searching by Rayyan id works**: id = 716321889 + WS number, now verified on WS0001, WS0091, WS1875, WS1890 and WS2375 (716324264), i.e. also inside the patent block. The record shown: title 一种基于标准箱改进的活框土养蜂箱, abstract beginning 本实用新型提供一种基于标准箱改进的活框土养蜂箱，包括标准框、纱盖、箱盖和中空加高框 = RIS record WS2375 (TY PAT), a clean E5. Instructions given: Exclude → type `E5 not a formal article` → confirm it is ticked → Apply (as for E1–E4); check the red reason chip and Excluded; then clear the search (× on the Search chip); do not touch Analyze or AI Reviewer; tell Fei Peng that E5 exists and that she screens WS2375 herself like the other four; the id search is for locating records (creating reasons or labels), not for choosing what to screen. Manual item V7 is partly answered (id search works; Chinese-text search does not narrow the list). This decision would be the first reviewer's fifth deliberate decision made to create a reason; the protocol v1.4 and amendment texts say four decisions on 7 October to create four reasons and leave exact dates and counts to the Rayyan log, which stays true. **Not yet confirmed that E5 was created.** For the next revision: add the id-search method to the manual (chapters 7–8, V7) and the E2 row for unrelated non-medical articles to guide 4.3.

**11 Oct 2026 (later still): the author asked what exactly E5 `E5 not a formal article` excludes ("详细说一下这个排除原因排除的内容是什么？").** Answered from guide 4.3/5/8, the registered eligibility text ("Excluded: reviews without new patients (used only for reference checking), conference abstracts, and editorials without patient data") and protocol v1.3 Section 14 ("conference papers with an abstract only are excluded as not a formal article"). E5 is a record-type reason: patents, newspapers, product descriptions, abstract-only conference records; not topic-based (an irrelevant bee-keeping journal article is E2; a bee-keeping patent is E5). Counts of non-journal types in `records_deduplicated.ris`: PAT 50 (all Chinese, WS2375–WS2426 block; 46 of them contain 蜂 or 箱, the rest include a spleen-fixation pack patent for trauma and an eyeglass patent), NEWS 3 (Chinese, no abstract), CONF 14 (2 English, 12 Chinese, all with an abstract text), THES 10 (Chinese; clinical data → Maybe, animal/lab → E1), CHAP 20 (English), GEN 21 (Chinese; a database label, not E5 by itself); no record is labelled as a product description. Gaps flagged to the author, **not yet in the guide and not approved**: (1) guide 4.3 words the conference rule as "只有摘要没有个体数据" (reads like a content test), whereas the registered text and v1.3 are format-based (abstract-only conference records are excluded) — align the wording at the next guide revision; for a record that cannot be classified, Maybe applies; (2) book chapters (CHAP, 20 English records) are not covered anywhere — Claude suggested E4 for textbook-style chapters without new cases and Maybe where the chapter reports the author's own patients; (3) the earlier candidate row for unrelated non-medical journal articles (→ E2). Also stated: any applicable reason is acceptable when two apply (a newspaper item about pig-to-monkey liver transplantation fits E1 and E5); E1–E6 remain usable at the full-text stage next to L, D, A, N. No document changed.

**11 Oct 2026 (later still): the author asked what `review-for-refs` means ("review-是什么意思？"; screenshot of one list item from the author's own account — chips "✕ 俊", red `E4 no original patient data`, blue `review-for-refs`; the record is WS0485, TY JOUR, 2013, with an abstract; no verdict on the decision was given).** Answered from guide section 3 / 4.3 / 8 item 4 and the registered eligibility text ("reviews without new patients (used only for reference checking)"): `review-for-refs` = "review, kept for its references", a **label** (blue chip), not an exclusion reason (red chip); used only together with E4 and only on reviews without new patients (a review that reports the authors' own patients is not E4 and gets no label); it does not change the decision, does not enter the PRISMA reason counts, and only lets the team list such reviews later to check their reference lists (backward citation searching, currently not planned after the 4 Oct decision; whether it is done must be stated in the paper). **Consequences:** the label now exists in the formal project (name as in the guide, created by the first reviewer on WS0485; the first reviewer has therefore screened at least that record); the second reviewer should pick it from the Label list, not retype it; E6 and `lang-other` still not created, E5 not confirmed. **Unverified:** whether blind mode hides labels from the other reviewer (the manual lists note visibility as V3 but not labels); if the second reviewer sees a blue label on WS0485 before deciding, it reveals the first reviewer's judgement for that record — asked the author to have her report it so it can be added to the Methods/Limitations. For the next guide revision: spell out "refs = references" next to the label name in section 3.

**Roadmap to a finished paper (author asked "完成论文，还需要做什么", 11 Oct 2026; Claude's proposal, nothing in it approved yet).** Facts below come from the repo (`records_deduplicated.ris`, protocol v1.4 `tools/build_protocol.js` Sections 5, 10, 14–18, Appendix C); nothing was read from PROSPERO or journal sites.
- **Where we are:** searches closed; title/abstract screening of 2,548 records running (both reviewers have started); full-text stage, IPD requests, extraction, risk of bias, analysis and manuscript are all not started. Own five-patient series and the EJP-version text/figures exist; no draft of the SR manuscript is recorded in these notes or in the repo.
- **Language mix of the 2,548 (RIS `LA` field):** English 1,446; Chinese 727; other 375 (15%: French 80, Russian 42, Spanish 48, German 54, Italian 41, Japanese 15, Portuguese 14, others) — protocol Section 10 says other languages are listed and counted, not extracted. **Years:** before 1970 193; 1970–89 315; 1990–99 314; 2000–09 497; 2010–19 640; 2020 on 530; 59 without year. **Crude full-text workload:** if the Include+Maybe share seen in the 50-record calibration (48–56%) holds for the 2,173 English/Chinese records, about 1,050–1,220 full texts would have to be retrieved and read (n = 50, so wide; the real number is known only after the title/abstract stage). Retrieval of old and Chinese full texts is the main bottleneck; consider whether a documented Maybe-triage step is needed (a deviation from the protocol, to be decided then, not now).
- **Update search is due:** see HANDOFF item (4). Embase is still unavailable, so the update would again cover six databases unless a licensed route appears.
- **Analysis software:** R is **not installed** in the container (only Python 3 and Node; Python has no `statsmodels`; numpy/scipy/pandas were not checked separately). The registered analysis (Section 16: one-stage logistic regression on log2 index-episode duration, cluster-robust SE by report, adjusted for age and publication period; spline only if ≥10 events per df; categories 2 d or less / 3–7 / 8–30 / over 30 d with exact CIs and trend test; bounding analyses for missing durations; 8 sensitivity analyses; subgroups GV/GOO, congenital vs acquired, diaphragmatic anomaly) needs R; scripts can be written here but not run here — Ji Wang would run them, or a Python cross-check can be built if the packages exist.
- **Proposed order (owner):** (1) now — Jun Shu submits the v1.4 PROSPERO amendment; E5 confirmed; Fei Peng screens, independently, the records used to create the reasons (WS0091, WS0157, WS0095, WS0689, and WS2375 once E5 exists); continue title/abstract screening (about 9–17 h per screener, crude); (2) both finish → unblind in the formal project → export → `tools/screening_conflicts.py` → kappa → discussion → Jun Yang adjudicates → list for full text; (3) full-text retrieval and screening (reasons L, D, A, N + E1–E6), IPD requests in one batch (two e-mails four weeks apart; 李拴玲 19-child and 赵娜 6-child series first checked); (4) extraction on the Appendix C form after calibration on 10 reports, two independent extractors, kappa for primary exposure and primary outcome, disagreements by discussion (protocol is silent on a tie-breaker for extraction — Claude suggests Jun Yang); (5) Murad risk-of-bias by two reviewers, GRADE narrative; (6) update search and its screening; (7) analysis (Ji Wang) with the script frozen before the final dataset is analysed; (8) manuscript, PRISMA 2020 / PRISMA-IPD / PRISMA-S checklists, PRISMA flow (identified 4,906 → 2,548 to screen, plus the update), figures, supplementary dataset (protocol promises the full IPD dataset), declarations; (9) update the PROSPERO record at completion. Crude calendar if everything goes well: title/abstract through Nov 2026; full-text retrieval and screening Dec 2026–Feb 2027; extraction and risk of bias Mar–Apr 2027; update search about Apr 2027; analysis May 2027; submission Jun–Jul 2027 (registered completion 28 Jun 2027) — planning numbers only.
- **Things Claude can prepare without waiting for screening (proposed):** an extraction workbook generated from Appendix C (controlled values, the nine duration-conversion rules as a guide, one row per patient); a full-text screening guide (the current guide covers title/abstract; section 6 only sketches full text); IPD request e-mails in Chinese and English; draft Methods, PRISMA-S search appendix and the deviations list from the logs in this file; the analysis script. **Needs the author, not Claude:** reading Orphanet J Rare Dis author guidelines (article type, abstract structure, word limit, declarations, current APC — the official pages were never read here); written consent for publication from the parents of all five children and for the images (not recorded in these notes — confirm); PACS exports of Case 5 images at print resolution (the current figures are 430–705 px); a decision on reference-list checking of included reports (the amendment note says the final report will state whether it was done); recheck of impact factors at submission.
- **Honest expectations to keep in the Discussion:** the registered primary analysis may be null or non-linear (the pilot showed a non-monotonic pattern; own losses came after short index episodes and fit the deferred-surgery secondary exposure); certainty will be very low; Embase, Google Scholar and citation searching were not done; other-language reports are not extracted.

**Extraction workbook and full-text screening guide drafted (11 Oct 2026; author approved starting with these two: "同意，从提取表和全文筛选指南开始"). Nothing in either is approved by the three reviewers yet.**
- **`Extraction workbook - template (draft).xlsx`** (v2026-10-11a; generator `tools/build_extraction_workbook.py`, needs `openpyxl`, `python3 -I tools/build_extraction_workbook.py "<out>.xlsx"`). Sheets: README, FT_log (full-text screening log, 15 columns, fallback if Rayyan's full-text feature is not used), Reports (17 columns, one row per report; `report_id` = WS id, own series = OWN), Patients (73 columns, one row per patient), IPD_requests (12 columns), Rules (the nine duration-conversion rules verbatim from protocol Appendix C with Chinese gloss, protocol definitions of the primary exposure/outcome, definitions of derived columns), Duration_calc (calculator for rules 1–4), Lists (all drop-down values). Three header rows (field code / Appendix C label / hint and allowed values), data from row 4; blank = not yet extracted, NR = not reported; rows pre-formatted: FT_log 2,000, Reports 800, Patients 1,500, IPD 300. Every Appendix C item has a column (checked item by item). **Additions beyond Appendix C, all listed on the README sheet as A1–A7 and awaiting approval:** report-level items split into a Reports sheet; associated conditions and imaging modalities split into Yes/No/NR columns, plus pancreatic-tail torsion and bowel obstruction (protocol Section 13); Procedure gets "No operation" (Section 7: children managed without operation are summarised descriptively), Fixation gets "Not applicable", several lists get "NR"/"Not applicable"; `ws_basis` (what the report relies on to call it a wandering spleen); `age_verbatim`, extractor/date, `same_patient_as`, duration-rule/lower-bound/imprecise-category columns (rules 5 and 9 ask for flags), source columns. **Derived (grey) columns are Claude's reading of the protocol:** `own_patient`; `idx_dur_category` (<=2 / 3-7 / 8-30 / >30 d; boundary values go to the lower class; non-integer days such as 2.5 fall in the next class — the protocol lists whole-day categories only); `thrombocytosis` (>450); `primary_outcome` (Non-viable if viability = Non-viable, or Partially non-viable with total/partial splenectomy; Viable stays Viable even if the spleen was removed; Partially non-viable without resection = Review; NR with a splenectomy = "NR - removed, check reason"); `pub_period` (before 2000 / 2000-2014 / 2015 onward). **Test:** formulas were evaluated with the Python `formulas` package on nine invented test rows covering each branch (not saved), all as designed; the container has no Excel and its LibreOffice has no Calc, so the file has **never been opened in Excel or WPS** and the drop-downs and layout are unverified (README says so). pip installs done in this container (ephemeral): openpyxl, formulas, python-dateutil, pymupdf; note `python3 -I` cannot see the user-site packages in `/root/.local`.
- **`Full-text screening guide (draft).md/.docx`** (v2026-10-11a, protocol v1.4; generated with `tools/md_to_docx.js`, validate.py passed, 252 of 252 text segments of the .md found in the .docx, layout previewed only through pandoc → Chromium → PyMuPDF). Fourteen sections: scope, one-page flow, tools (Rayyan Full text screening tab is **unverified**, FT_log as fallback; PDFs kept out of the repo), retrieval (language triage by RIS language field: English 1,446, Chinese 727, other 375 = reason L, not retrieved), reasons and a **fixed order for choosing one reason per report** (L, D, E5, E4, E1, E2, E3, A, N, E6), question-by-question decision rules, 26-row situation table, aggregate-only reports and IPD requests (Pending IPD → Include or Exclude A; second e-mail followed by a further 4 weeks; request only Appendix C variables, no names/IDs/dates; data kept outside the repo), FT_log columns, disagreement handling, PRISMA numbers, a proposed 20-report calibration, and eight open decisions **D1–D8** with Claude's recommendation: D1 paediatric "splenic torsion" with no position/ligament information — recommended lenient with `ws_basis` flag (alternative: strict E2); D2 "child/小儿/患儿" without a number counts as under 18, age NR ("adolescent" = N); D3 the reason order; D4 Rayyan names `L other language`, `D no full text`, `A aggregate data only`, `N age not confirmed under 18` and how to show Pending IPD (Maybe + label `ipd-pending`, or FT_log only); D5 calibration of 20 reports; D6 four further weeks after the second IPD e-mail; D7 report full-text kappa; D8 data from review tables are not extracted, reference checking of `review-for-refs` reviews decided separately. **This guide supersedes manual chapter 11; the manual (.md/.docx) was not changed — point chapter 11 to the new guide at its next revision.**
- **Not done / next (proposed, not started):** IPD request e-mails (Chinese and English); Methods draft and PRISMA-S appendix; an extraction comparison script (disagreement list and kappa — protocol says kappa for primary exposure and primary outcome but not how for a continuous exposure; suggested: kappa on the four duration categories plus missing, and on viability, with days reported as agreement within a tolerance); RoB sheet (Murad items per protocol Section 15) and the analysis script (R is not installed in the container).

**Formal title/abstract screening finished by both reviewers; export received and analysed (12 Oct 2026). Findings need an explanation from Fei Peng before adjudication — nothing decided yet.** The author unblinded the formal project (Blind Mode off) and exported with Review data > "···" > Export (the "Review settings" dialog has no export; it only has General/Archive/Delete; in the Export dialog the default File format was Refman and had to be changed to CSV; All references, abstracts/decisions/reasons/labels/notes ticked). Raw files `articles.csv` (2,548 rows) and `customizations_log.csv` (10,109 events; contain reviewer e-mails) were uploaded to the chat only and are **not** in the repo. Saved in `search/screening/formal/` (no e-mails; grep checked): `screening_results_all.csv`, `screening_conflicts_for_adjudication.csv`, `screening_include_vs_maybe.csv`, `screening_summary.txt` (from `tools/screening_conflicts.py`), `formal_decisions_log_deidentified.csv` (event log with R1/R2 and WS ids only) and `formal_log_findings.txt` (output of the new `tools/formal_log_analysis.py RAW_DIR OUT_DIR`). All 2,548 records matched to WS ids (1,601 by DOI, 801 by URL, 145 by title+year+author, 1 by title+year+volume+page); both reviewers have 0 undecided; the log and the export agree on every decision (0 differences); the two log users map one-to-one to the two display names (2,548 of 2,548 decisions each).
- **Results.** R1 (Jun Shu): Exclude 1,716 / Maybe 512 / Include 320. R2 (Fei Peng): Exclude 1,186 / Maybe 1,089 / Include 273. 3-category agreement 69.7%, kappa 0.483; advance (Include or Maybe) vs Exclude agreement 74.8%, kappa 0.508 — far below the calibration (90.0%, 0.83). Categories: agree-Exclude 1,130; agree-Include 206; agree-Maybe 439; Include-vs-Maybe 131; **advance-vs-exclude conflicts 642** (25%; the calibration-based estimate was about 200, range 80–480): 586 are R1 Exclude vs R2 Maybe (544) or Include (42), 56 are R2 Exclude vs R1 Maybe (48) or Include (8). Agreement is similarly low in the first 180 list positions (3-category 71.1%, kappa 0.48) and in the rest (69.6%, kappa 0.48), so the low agreement is not only a late-phase effect: R2 advances 54% of records after position 180 against R1's 32%.
- **R1 deviations from the guide:** 310 Excludes carry **no exclusion reason** (guide section 3: no E-reason → choose Maybe); 129 of them conflict with an R2 advance (R2 Maybe 119, Include 10), 181 are agreed Excludes; all single (not bulk) decisions, WS0001–WS1638, mostly JOUR. Three records carry Rayyan default reasons (WS0584 and WS0609 `background article`, WS0801 `wrong publication type`; guide says never use defaults). On 10 Oct 02:33 UTC R1 applied E5 to WS0001 (a 19-year-old woman's case report) and undid it within 15 s; the E1 stray label of 7 Oct appears as 2 add/delete pairs. R1's pace: median 9.4 s between first decisions, about 7.5 h of active time (gaps ≤120 s) over 5 days, first decisions in list order on 10–12 Oct.
- **R2 account — facts from the log (interpretation deliberately not given; explanation requested).** (1) **Pace:** first about 176 records (10 Oct 05–07 UTC, i.e. 13–15 h China time) at median 9.5–14.8 s per record, similar to the calibration; after that median **1.6–2.2 s** between consecutive first decisions (1,007 records in one UTC hour on 11 Oct 03:xx and 1,180 in the next; overall median 1.86 s, 10th–90th percentile 1.5–3.6 s, 2,232 of 2,547 gaps ≤3 s, none ≤1 s; about 2.1 h active time for 2,548 records); strictly in list order (2,523 of 2,547 steps are WS+1); the same speed for Exclude, Maybe and Include. (2) **Bulk actions:** 942 of R2's 1,186 final Excludes were set in multi-select batches (up to 50 records within one second, 61 such seconds, 11 Oct 00:06–13:22 UTC) with reasons E1/E2/E3/E5; 919 of these 942 match R1's Exclude (97.6%), 21 R1 Maybe and 2 R1 Include conflict. (3) **Label `AI-prescreen`:** applied by the R2 account to 179 records (12 on 10 Oct 16:45 UTC, then batches of 50/50/50/17 on 11 Oct 00:06–00:11 UTC), all removed again between 11 Oct 01:16 and 01:26 UTC; R2's decisions on those 179: Exclude 74, Maybe 72, Include 33 (R1: Exclude 125, Include 29, Maybe 25). The label name is not Claude's; **what it means, who or what created it and whether any AI tool (Rayyan's AI Reviewer or an external one) was used is unknown and was asked.** Protocol v1.3/v1.4 Section 14 and the amendment note state that Rayyan's AI functions are not used — **hold the PROSPERO v1.4 amendment until this is clarified** (it also still says screening "not completed" and promises exact dates, which are now available). R2 also used the `review-for-refs` label 25 times (R1 117). R2's E-reasons: E1 178, E2 502, E3 419, E4 32, E5 55 (none missing); R1 among Excludes (combinations counted in each): E1 236, E2 702, E3 322, E4 120, E5 60, plus the 3 default-reason records; 26 R1 records whose final decision is Include (16) or Maybe (10) still carry a leftover exclusion reason (they were presumably changed from Exclude; the export keeps the reason).
- **Dates for Methods (UTC; China time +8).** R1 first formal decision 7 Oct 13:20:56 (the seeded record WS0001; seeded records 7 Oct), last 12 Oct 12:13:52. R2 first decision 7 Oct 14:06:49 (4 decisions that day), screening proper from 10 Oct 05:11, last decision 11 Oct 14:05:12. Blind Mode was switched off by the author on 12 Oct (after the last decision). Per-reviewer own counts before unblinding: asked, not yet answered (the author's "舒俊的" reply carried no numbers).
- **Options put to the author (none chosen):** A — R2 re-screens all 2,548 independently in a fresh blind project (R1's original decisions stay; comparison by script; R2 must not look at R1's decisions; about 8–15 h at R1's pace); B — Jun Yang screens the 642 conflicts blind to both decisions as the third reviewer (documented as such), and the dubious independence of R2's other decisions is reported as a limitation; C — keep both reviewers' decisions, adjudicate the 642, report pace/bulk/label facts as limitations (not recommended unless Fei Peng's explanation shows the decisions were genuinely individual). Conservative fallback that needs no adjudication: advance everything either reviewer advanced (1,418 records = 776 agreed advances + 642 conflicts), exclude only agreed Excludes (1,130) — heavier full-text workload (the 586 R1-Exclude conflicts are mostly expected to be irrelevant). If R1's 310 reason-less Excludes were treated as Maybe (guide rule), 129 conflicts would become agreements (513 conflicts remain). Reported in the Methods whatever is chosen: bulk actions, pace, the AI-prescreen label, default reasons, reason-less Excludes, and the screenshot exposure of 11 Oct (R1 saw R2's decisions on WS0001–WS0010 positions in a screenshot Claude had asked for; R1's own decision on WS0001 was already made on 10 Oct 07:26 UTC, before the 11 Oct screenshot, so that exposure did not affect that record).

**14 Oct 2026: author's question "两个人，谁的错误更多，错的多的重新做" (whoever made more errors redoes it) — Claude's data-based answer; no redo decided.** The log has no gold standard, so accuracy was checked against the **45 pilot papers with IPD** (`literature-pilot-extraction-53-papers.csv`; all are eligible paediatric wandering-spleen reports and must advance). Mapping of the 53 pilot papers to WS ids by first author, year and title (checked by reading titles) is in `tools/pilot_reference_check.py`; per-paper decisions in `search/screening/formal/pilot_reference_set_decisions.csv`. **Results:** R1 (Jun Shu) Excluded 5 of 45 (11.1%, Wilson 95% CI 4.8–23.5%; WS0681 Esposito 2005 and WS0601 Ben Ely 2008 with reason `E1 non-human`, WS0537 Magno 2011 `E2`, WS0321 Cohen 2018 `E4`, WS0186 Nakagawa 2022 `E3 adults only`); R2 (Fei Peng) Excluded 1 of 45 (2.2%, CI 0.4–11.6%; WS0168 Liao 2022 letter, `E1 non-human`); Fisher exact p = 0.20, i.e. **not statistically distinguishable at n = 45**. No pilot paper was excluded by both; "advance if either advances" would have missed 0 of 45. These papers were found by hand and are probably easier to recognise than the average eligible record, so the rates are optimistic. **Other measurable differences:** guide-compliance errors are all R1's (310 reason-less Excludes, 3 default reasons, 26 leftover reasons; R2 none). `E1 non-human` use: R1 236 records, of which R2 advanced 43 (18%) and excluded 85 with another reason; R2 178 records, of which R1 advanced 5 (3%) — suggests R1 mis-selected reasons (possibly mis-clicks) more often. R2's 23 bulk Excludes that R1 advanced (WS list in the findings): about 10 are accessory-spleen papers (R2 right under the guide), about 12 are bulk `E3 adults only` on titles that do not state an adult age (e.g. WS2214 游走脾2例报告 1993, WS2256 先天性游走脾并发脾蒂扭转2例 1987, WS1530, WS1705, WS0420) — possible false exclusions by bulk action. **Interpretation given:** on measurable accuracy R1 looks worse (more false exclusions, all guide-compliance errors), but R2's open question is integrity (2 s per record, bulk actions, `AI-prescreen` label), not accuracy, and cannot be scored as "errors"; the two reviewers' errors are mostly different records, so adjudication/union recovers them (the reference set: 45/45). Unblinded re-screening by either reviewer is no longer independent. **Recommendation given:** do not redo all 2,548; (1) get Fei Peng's explanation (still pending, not yet reported by the author); (2) Jun Yang judges the 642 conflicts blind to both decisions (about 3–4 h; or a random 100 first) and each reviewer's error count against his judgement decides who redoes what — only the unreliable subset (R1: the 310 reason-less and the E1 records; R2: whatever the explanation shows), not everything; (3) if Fei Peng's explanation shows her decisions were not her own independent reading, she re-screens all 2,548 in a fresh blind project (option A of the 12 Oct paragraph).

**14 Oct 2026: author asked how to solve the screening-quality problem and approved the plan "blind third-reviewer judgement + quality-control sample" ("同意，生成盲评表"); no one redoes the full screening.** Plan as proposed and approved: (1) Fei Peng is asked three questions (what `AI-prescreen` is; how about 2 s per record was done on 11 Oct; how the 942 bulk exclusions were selected) — answer still pending, not yet reported by the author; (2) Jun Yang judges a blind sheet of **873 records** (about 4–5 h, in several sessions): all **642 conflicts**, **117 both-excluded records that look relevant** (a wandering/torsion word and a child word in title or abstract), **54 both-excluded records without abstract** (not flagged), and a **random QC sample of 60** of the other 800 both-excluded records; 159 both-excluded records are left out as clear by type (both reviewers gave E1, or both gave E5); (3) **pre-specified triggers**: if Jun Yang advances more than 10% of the 171 targeted records, or 2 or more of the 60 random QC records, he also judges all remaining both-excluded records with E2 or E3 (about 690 more); (4) Claude then counts each reviewer's errors against his judgement (each conflict is one error for the reviewer he disagrees with; false exclusions counted separately), gives exact intervals, and writes the final full-text list = records both advanced (776) + conflicts he advanced + QC records he advanced; (5) the reviewer with more errors does a 30-record recalibration with the other before the full-text stage (instead of redoing everything), after which the guide's Maybe criteria are aligned (the main driver of the 586 R1-Exclude/R2-advance conflicts is R2's 43% Maybe rate against R1's 20%); (6) methods text and PROSPERO amendment (protocol v1.5) to be written after his results, stating all facts (pace, bulk actions, `AI-prescreen`, reason-less Excludes, default reasons, screenshot exposure, unblinding, export) and the remedy; the sentence "Rayyan's AI functions are not used" depends on Fei Peng's answer; (7) preventive rules for the full-text stage and the update search: export after the first 100 records of each stage to check agreement and pace, no bulk exclusion unless the filter is written down first, a daily script check that every Exclude has a reason, both reviewers keep a start/end time log. **Files (in `search/screening/formal/`):** `blind_adjudication_sheet.xlsx` (the file for Jun Yang: sheet 说明 with a summary of the rules, sheet 判断 with blind id, year, language, title, abstract, dropdown columns for decision and reason, sheet 时间日志; it holds no WS ids and no decisions; 873 records in random order, seed 20261014; the xlsx was only reloaded with openpyxl, never opened in Excel/WPS), `blind_adjudication_key.csv` (blind id to WS id, stratum, both reviewers' decisions — **must not be given to Jun Yang**), `blind_adjudication_design.txt`; scripts `tools/make_blind_adjudication_sheet.py` and `tools/blind_adjudication_analysis.py FILLED.xlsx OUT_DIR` (tested on invented random decisions in the scratchpad only — those outputs are not saved). Records are matched to the guide: Jun Yang uses the same Include/Maybe/Exclude and E1–E6 names; his Exclude without a reason is flagged by the script.

**15 Oct 2026: Fei Peng's answers to the three questions (relayed by the author in one short message; the wording is ambiguous and the details below are asked, not established).** (1) **`AI-prescreen`: "写错了，可以不要这个标签"** — i.e. the label was written wrongly and can be dropped; it is already gone from the project (removed from all 179 records on 11 Oct 01:16–01:26 UTC, per the log). What the label meant (a wrong name, or a mark that an AI had pre-screened those 179 records) was not stated. (2) **"11日每条约2秒及942条批量修复用codex"** — the ~2 s per record on 11 Oct and the 942 bulk exclusions were done with **Codex** (an AI coding agent; presumably driving Rayyan). This matches the log pattern (about 2,367 of R2's 2,548 first decisions — the 12 on 10 Oct 16:45 UTC and about 2,355 from 11 Oct 00:04 to 14:05 UTC — at 1.5–3.6 s, in list order, with 50-record batches), while the first about 180 first decisions (10 Oct 05–09 UTC, at 9–15 s each) look like human reading. **Consequence stated to the author:** R2's decisions on about 93% of the records cannot be treated as a second reviewer's independent reading; the protocol (Section 14) requires two independent reviewers, and the statement "Rayyan's AI functions are not used" in protocol v1.3/v1.4 is literally about Rayyan's own AI and is not the issue — the issue is the missing independent human screening, which must be disclosed in the Methods and the PROSPERO amendment (v1.5). **Open questions put to the author (not yet answered):** (a) did Codex make the Include/Maybe/Exclude judgements (reading title and abstract), or only enter decisions Fei Peng had made herself elsewhere; (b) which instructions and inputs Codex received (guide or her own criteria), and whether she read its output and how many she changed; (c) what "942 批量修复" repaired and which records changed; (d) whether the first about 180 records were hers and everything after was Codex; (e) **she should save now** the Codex conversation/run log, prompts, scripts, model name and version, date/time and the output files (needed for the Methods and reproducibility). **Plan status:** the blind third-reviewer sheet (873 records) is **on hold** — do not send it to Jun Yang until (a) is answered, because the conflict set will change if Fei Peng re-screens; if it was already sent, ask how far he has got. **Recommendation given:** if Codex made the judgements (most likely, given the pace and the label), Fei Peng re-screens the about 2,370 AI-decided records herself, by reading, without any AI, in a fresh blind Rayyan project (R1's decisions unseen), with her own start/end time log and an interim export after the first 100 records (about 7–10 h at R1's pace); the Codex decisions are kept as a separate, documented "AI screening" dataset (later comparison with the human decisions is a legitimate by-product, reported as such); conflicts are then recomputed and go to Jun Yang blind as already designed (a new sheet, probably smaller than 642). If Codex only entered her own prior judgements, her decisions stand as hers (she must supply the original judgement file with times), the blind sheet goes ahead as designed, and the automation is disclosed. Using Codex as a standing second screener is not recommended: it is not in the protocol and its sensitivity is only supported by the 45-paper reference set (1 false exclusion in 45). **Also on hold:** the PROSPERO amendment (v1.4 text now outdated; next version v1.5 will state the facts above once (a)–(d) are answered).

**16 Oct 2026: replacing reviewer 2 with Ji Wang was considered and dropped; reviewers stay Jun Shu and Fei Peng.** The author first asked whether Fei Peng could be removed from the Rayyan project so that Ji Wang (statistician, already on the PROSPERO team list) would do the screening. Advice given: (i) removal would be by Revoke (keeps decisions, labels and notes visible; per Rayyan help-centre search summaries only, page not read) rather than Delete, and nothing in the old project is deleted or archived; (ii) Ji Wang must NOT be added to the formal project, which has been unblinded since 12 Oct and would show both reviewers' decisions — a fresh blind project would be needed, with Jun Shu's original decisions kept as reviewer 1 and the 873-record blind sheet rebuilt; (iii) the swap would be a registered-protocol deviation (reviewers Jun Shu and Fei Peng) for the v1.5 amendment. The author then decided the same day to **keep Jun Shu and Fei Peng**; Ji Wang was not invited and nothing was changed in Rayyan. **The Codex question is therefore unchanged and still blocks everything downstream:** Fei Peng's answers (a)–(e) from the 15 Oct paragraph are outstanding; the blind sheet for Jun Yang and the PROSPERO amendment (v1.5) stay on hold. **Next-step plan given (proposals, not yet approved):** (1) Fei Peng saves the Codex evidence and answers (a)–(e). (2) If Codex made the judgements: Fei Peng re-screens by reading, without any AI, in a fresh blind Rayyan project (same `records_deduplicated.ris`, Blind ON, E1–E6 recreated, keyword lists reset, author/journal hidden; Jun Shu owns it, does not screen there and does not unblind; the old project is not opened); scope changed from the earlier "about 2,370 records" to **all 2,548** — the earlier cut-off at about the first 176 records was inferred from decision pace, not confirmed by Fei Peng, and one uniform procedure is easier to report (cost about 30 min); rules: no Codex or other AI, no bulk Exclude, every Exclude with a reason, start/end time log, export after the first 100 records for a pace/agreement check (about 7–10 h in total); the Codex decisions stay as a separate documented "AI screening" dataset and are not used for the final list. If Codex only entered decisions Fei Peng had already made herself: she supplies the original judgement file with times, her decisions stand with disclosure, and the 873-record sheet goes to Jun Yang as designed. (3) After the re-screen: export, adapt `tools/screening_conflicts.py` to compare decisions from two projects (Jun Shu's old project vs Fei Peng's new one; match by WS id), rebuild the blind sheet (conflicts + word-flagged and no-abstract both-excluded records + random QC sample, same triggers), Jun Yang judges blind, `tools/blind_adjudication_analysis.py` counts each reviewer's errors and writes the full-text list. **Pre-specification proposed before any new decision exists:** Jun Shu's 310 Excludes without a reason are treated as Maybe in the comparison (guide section 3 rule) and his recorded decisions are not edited, because he has seen the other reviewer's decisions since unblinding. (4) In parallel: protocol v1.5 and PROSPERO amendment drafts with all facts (conditional on the answers), IPD request e-mails, extraction comparison script, RoB sheet; low-regret preparation of full-text retrieval for the 832 records Jun Shu advanced (320 Include + 512 Maybe; retrieval creates no decisions; language reason L applies to other-language records).

**16 Oct 2026 (later): per-reviewer problem check of the formal screening (author: "彭飞和舒俊的筛查表各自有什么问题？").** New script `tools/reviewer_issue_check.py` (stdlib only; `python3 -I tools/reviewer_issue_check.py` from the repo root) writes `search/screening/formal/reviewer_issue_check.txt` (summary) and `reviewer_issue_records.csv` (1,332 flag rows: ws_id, reviewer, issue, decision, reason, title; no e-mails). Issue class **A = mechanical guide breaches (solid)**; classes B, C, G are **keyword proxies and are noisy** — e.g. the "child cue" flag on R2's bulk E3 Excludes was a false alarm when abstracts were read (adults aged 22–52, post-partum), so B/C/G rows are pointers only. Samples were read by Claude (single reader, not adjudicated). **R1 Jun Shu:** (1) 310 of 1,716 Excludes carry no reason (guide: choose Maybe), concentrated in one session on 10 Oct 07–10 UTC (41–66% of that hour's Excludes without a reason, WS0001–WS1638; none before 07:00 or after about 10:30 UTC), 129 of them conflict with an R2 advance; (2) reason errors: E1 non-human used 240 times, 57 on records whose text has no animal word — reading samples, most are human cases (e.g. WS1553 infant splenic/pancreatic torsion, WS0266, WS1120), only a few truly non-human (crab, bee-keeping); of the 5 pilot papers R1 excluded, 2 carry E1 (Esposito 2005, Ben Ely 2008, both human paediatric); E3 adults only used 329 times, text supports an adult in 185 (56%), and E3/E2 were put on titles that say child/infant/neonate (WS0002, WS0734, WS0789, WS0840, WS1283, WS1731, WS0526, WS0928); (3) false exclusions: among 130 records whose TITLE names a wandering/ectopic spleen and a child cue (title-only strict list), R1 excluded 23; excluding the plainly adult or letter ones, at least 12 are clearly eligible by title (WS0002, 0526, 0734, 0789, 0840, 0916, 0928, 0945, 0947, 0982, 1283, 1731; borderline WS0187, WS0839, WS1115) and R2 advanced all 12; pilot reference set 5/45 excluded (11.1%, CI 4.8–23.5%); (4) other breaches: 3 Rayyan default reasons (WS0584, WS0609, WS0801), 26 Include/Maybe with a leftover reason, 36 Excludes with two or more reasons, 47 Includes on records without abstract (guide: Maybe/Exclude only; the 10 inspected were all paediatric wandering-spleen titles, so no practical effect), 59 reason-less Excludes among 257 no-abstract Excludes; 49 records had a changed decision. Pace normal (median 9.4 s, about 7.5 h). **R2 Fei Peng:** (1) process: first 181 records (first decision before 10 Oct 10:00 UTC, human pace) R2 advanced 35.4% vs R1 39.2% on the same records; after that (2,367 records, about 2 s each) R2 advanced 54.8% vs R1 32.2% — the behaviour changed at the point where the pace changed (3-category agreement similar in both parts, kappa 0.48 and 0.48); (2) first decisions were Maybe for 1,442 of 2,548 records (57%), and 355 records changed Maybe to Exclude, 340 of them inside bulk seconds, median 4.6 h (range 0.6–22.9 h) after the first Maybe — consistent with a two-pass workflow (broad first pass, then bulk exclusion with reasons = the "942 批量修复"), not proof; (3) over-inclusion: 1,089 Maybe (43%), of which 139 have no wandering/torsion word in the text and 23 have an animal word and no child cue; (4) bulk Excludes (about 942–945, 919–922 agree with R1): samples read looked correct (dogs, accessory spleen, splenic autotransplantation, adults 22–52 years, post-partum) — no clear error found in about 35 read; (5) E1 errors: 13 E1 on records with no animal word, 8 of them single (non-bulk) clicks on human wandering-spleen case reports in WS0128–WS0251 (WS0128, 0162, 0163, 0168, 0171, 0175, 0213, 0251), two of which are clear false exclusions (WS0168 and WS0175, paediatric wandering-spleen titles; R1 had advanced both), the other 5 flagged are truly non-human (fish, chick, dog, prion cells); pilot reference set 1/45 excluded (2.2%, CI 0.4–11.6%, WS0168); (6) guide form is clean (0 reason-less Excludes, 0 default reasons, 0 leftover reasons) because reasons were applied in the bulk steps. **Common:** wrong E1 is the most frequent reason error for both reviewers; mechanism not established — R1's 57 wrong E1 are not explained by a carried-over reason (only 4 of 57 followed an E1 event, below the 16% base share); the position of E1 in the reason list or a hotkey is a guess, not verified. **Answer to "who made more errors":** on measurable errors R1 has more (form breaches, wrong reasons, at least 12 clear false exclusions); R2's problems are over-inclusion and the independence of the process (Codex), not accuracy of the exclusions. **Consequences:** recorded decisions are NOT to be edited (independence; adjudication handles them); the blind-sheet plan and the Codex questions (a)–(e) stay as in the 15–16 Oct paragraphs; the flag CSV can seed the recalibration examples for both reviewers.

**16 Oct 2026 (later still): the author reversed the attribution of the AI use — "说错了，舒俊用了AI，彭飞没用。舒俊重做，彭飞不重做，可以吗?" — which does not match the event log; NOT accepted as stated, one fact asked.** Check on the raw log (kept outside the repo; only account-level facts are recorded here, no e-mail address): the log has two user ids. **User A** — registered with the Gmail address of the Rayyan owner (Jun Shu's account, display name 俊): 4,387 events, median 9.4 s between first decisions, **0 seconds with 5 or more decisions, 0 `AI-prescreen` events**, first decision 7 Oct 13:20, last 12 Oct 12:13 UTC. **User B** — registered with a QQ Mail address (the address the author supplied on 5 Oct for Fei Peng's own Rayyan account, display name Fei): 5,722 events, **median 1.9 s between first decisions, 61 seconds with 5 or more decisions (bulk), 358 `AI-prescreen` events**, first decision 7 Oct 14:06 UTC. The mapping of users to display names is confirmed twice (by decisions, 2,548 of 2,548 each, and by e-mail provider). So the fast, bulk and `AI-prescreen` activity is entirely in the account registered under Fei Peng's own QQ address; the log shows which ACCOUNT acted, not which PERSON sat at the keyboard or ran Codex. Consequences stated to the author: (i) "Jun Shu redoes, Fei Peng does not" cannot fix the problem if it means Jun Shu re-doing his own account's decisions — those are not the fast/bulk decisions; (ii) a valid design needs two different humans; if Jun Shu ran Codex inside Fei Peng's account, the second-reviewer slot is empty (Fei Peng's own judgement is missing for about 93% of records and Jun Shu would be both reviewers), Fei Peng must screen herself in a fresh blind project and the account sharing is disclosed in the Methods and the PROSPERO amendment; (iii) if Fei Peng ran Codex, the earlier plan stands (Fei Peng re-screens by reading); (iv) if Jun Shu used an AI tool as an adviser for his own decisions while clicking by hand, the log cannot show it — then his R1 decisions are not independent human reading either and he re-screens, and the Fei-account pattern still needs its own explanation. **Question put to the author (unanswered):** who operated the Fei (QQ) account and who ran Codex on 10–11 Oct, and in what way did Jun Shu use AI (his own account? which tool? for which records? before or after clicking?). Evidence to save regardless: the Codex run log, prompts, scripts, model/version, times, outputs. All holds stay: blind sheet for Jun Yang, PROSPERO amendment (v1.5), no edits to recorded decisions.

**16 Oct 2026 (end of day): the author answered "彭飞自己运行了 Codex" — Fei Peng herself ran Codex in her own account (QQ-registered, display name Fei).** This matches the log and the 15 Oct statement; the same-day reversal "舒俊用了AI，彭飞没用" is not supported by the log and was not elaborated, so whether Jun Shu used any AI tool as an adviser for his own (hand-clicked, human-paced) decisions is asked once more. **Decision path now in force (the plan from the 15–16 Oct paragraphs):** Fei Peng re-screens all 2,548 records by reading, with no AI of any kind (no Codex, no Rayyan AI features), in a fresh blind Rayyan project (same `records_deduplicated.ris`; Blind ON; author/journal hidden; keyword lists reset to the guide's 19 green and 47 red words; E1–E6 and labels created by Fei Peng while screening, since creating a reason is a real Exclude decision; the old project is not opened). The Fei-account decisions of 10–11 Oct are kept as a separate documented "Codex-assisted" dataset (`screening_results_all.csv` columns R2_*, de-identified log) and are not used for the final list. Rules for the re-screen: no bulk Exclude, every Exclude with a reason, start/end time log, export after the first 100 records (Claude checks pace and aggregate agreement only; per-record agreement is not fed back to Fei Peng), about 7–10 h in total. Holds unchanged: blind sheet for Jun Yang (to be rebuilt from the new comparison), PROSPERO amendment (protocol v1.5). To build when the first-100 export arrives: a comparison script for two projects (Jun Shu old project vs Fei Peng new project, matched by WS id; Jun Shu's 310 reason-less Excludes treated as Maybe, proposed rule awaiting approval).

**16 Oct 2026 (end of day): PRE-SPECIFIED RULE APPROVED by the author ("同意，310条按Maybe处理"), fixed before any re-screen decision exists.** In the comparison of Jun Shu's recorded decisions with Fei Peng's re-screen, Jun Shu's **310 Excludes without any exclusion reason are treated as Maybe** (screening guide section 3: an Exclude needs an applicable E1–E6, otherwise choose Maybe). His recorded decisions are NOT edited in Rayyan or in any file; the recorded decision is kept in a separate column and a sensitivity analysis on the recorded decisions is always printed next to the main one. The 3 records with a Rayyan default reason (WS0584, WS0609, WS0801) are not covered by the rule and stay as recorded Excludes; they are mentioned in the Methods. **Correction of an earlier statement:** the 15–16 Oct notes said that with the rule "129 conflicts would become agreements (513 conflicts remain)". That ignored the other 181 reason-less Excludes that the second reviewer also excluded: under the rule they become Maybe-vs-Exclude conflicts. Using the Codex-assisted decisions as a stand-in for the re-screen (simulation only, not a prediction) the rule gives 694 conflicts instead of 642 (181 caused by the rule), i.e. a larger blind sheet for Jun Yang (877 records in the simulation); with the real re-screen the numbers will differ, but the direction is the same: the rule moves undocumented Excludes to blind adjudication. **New script `tools/compare_rescreen.py`** (stdlib only; tested on a simulated single-reviewer export built from the raw export, full and first-100, results match the old analysis exactly when the stand-in equals the old decisions): `python3 -I tools/compare_rescreen.py NEW_ARTICLES_CSV NEW_LOG_CSV search/dedup/records_deduplicated.ris search/screening/formal/screening_results_all.csv search/screening/rescreen --name Fei`. It matches the new project's records to WS ids, finds the deciding user in the log (identity only in memory), applies the rule, and writes `screening_results_all.csv` (same column names as the old file plus `R1_recorded_decision`, `R1_rule_applied`, `R2_codex_decision`, `R2_codex_reason`), the conflict list, the include-vs-maybe list and `rescreen_summary.txt` with agreement and kappa for the effective and the recorded Jun Shu decisions and a **process check of the re-screen** (median gap between decisions, share of gaps of 3 s or less, bulk seconds with 5 or more decisions, labels set, pace per 100 records). It works on partial exports, so it serves the first-100 check; the first 100 records should show a human pace (Jun Shu's median 9.4 s, Fei Peng's own first 181 records about 13 s; the Codex-assisted part 1.9 s). `tools/make_blind_adjudication_sheet.py` and `tools/blind_adjudication_analysis.py` now take an optional folder argument (default `search/screening/formal/`, behaviour unchanged — the regenerated key is byte-identical to the committed one) so that the blind sheet for Jun Yang can be rebuilt from `search/screening/rescreen/` after the re-screen. **Status:** waiting for Fei Peng's answers (a)–(e), Jun Shu's own AI use, and the new Rayyan project; nothing in the repo has been changed in the old results.

**17 Oct 2026: the open attribution question is closed by the author — "舒俊没有用过AI，彭飞用Codex做了判断".** Two facts now recorded: (1) **Jun Shu used no AI tool at any point**, so his recorded decisions (R1, human pace, hand-clicked) stand as independent human screening; the earlier same-day statement that he had used AI is withdrawn by the author. (2) **Fei Peng used Codex to MAKE the Include/Maybe/Exclude judgements** (not merely to enter decisions made elsewhere), i.e. the answer to question (a) is "Codex judged". Therefore **path A of the 16 Oct plan is in force**: Fei Peng re-screens all 2,548 records by reading, without any AI, in a fresh blind Rayyan project; Jun Shu does not redo anything; the Codex-assisted decisions of the Fei account (10–11 Oct, about 93% of the records plus the first part at human pace) stay as a separate documented dataset and are not used for the final list; the pre-specified rule (310 reason-less Excludes of Jun Shu treated as Maybe) applies in the comparison. For comparison: Jun Shu median 9.4 s per record; the Codex-assisted account 1.9 s. **What the log can and cannot show:** it shows when the server received each decision, not who or what produced it; a median of 0.3–0.5 s per record including the reason dialog excludes reading title and abstract at the moment of entry; the mechanism is not established (a macro, script or browser automation; decisions made elsewhere and keyed in; another tool; a client-side sync artifact is unlikely but not excluded). **The author's statement that no AI was used is not contradicted or confirmed by the log; the decisions were not read-and-clicked.** The decisions are not a plain keyword rule (agreement with a keyword rule 0.63, lower than Jun Shu's agreement with her), they differ from the Codex-assisted decisions (agreement 0.77, kappa 0.60, so not a replay of that file), and they contain content-dependent reasons. **Provisional numbers (not to be used):** re-screen Include 224 / Maybe 892 / Exclude 1,432; labels `lang-other` 86, `review-for-refs` 10; reasons E2 806, E3 372, E1 191, E5 53, E4 10, E6 0; no Exclude without a reason, no default reason; 2 of the 45 pilot reports with IPD excluded (27 Include, 16 Maybe); against Jun Shu: 734 advance-vs-exclude conflicts with the pre-specified rule (250 caused by the rule), 544 on his recorded decisions; 3-category kappa 0.40 (rule applied) / 0.51 (recorded); the 100-record checkpoint was not kept (no stop after the first 100; the first 100 decisions took seconds). **Holds:** no blind sheet for Jun Yang, no adjudication, no full-text list, no Methods wording and no PROSPERO v1.5 until the author has the second reviewer's explanation (which tool, script or macro; where the decisions came from; scripts, files, prompts, any AI of any kind including non-Codex tools; whether someone else helped) and decides the next step. **Options put to the author (none chosen):** (1) get the explanation first; ### Orphanet Journal of Rare Diseases — submission guidelines read (17 Oct 2026; author uploaded the 23-page PDF "Submission guidelines | Orphanet Journal of Rare Diseases | Springer Nature Link", printed 2026/10/17 23:19, URL link.springer.com/journal/13023/submission-guidelines)

**Reading method and limits.** The PDF has no text layer, so all 23 pages were rendered to images and read by Claude (single reader). The general PDF contains only the general part of the guidelines; the article-type pages appear there only as link titles on p. 6. **The Research (14 pp.) and Review (13 pp.) pages were uploaded by the author later on 17 Oct 2026 (printed 23:33) and read the same way — see the paragraph "Research and Review pages read" below.** Still not seen: the BMC editorial policies (data availability, consent, ethics, authorship, AI use), the pages for Position statement, Meeting Report and Correspondence (not needed), and any statement of a main-text word limit or a limit on tables and figures (neither article-type page gives one).

**Facts taken from the PDF (page numbers in brackets):**
- *Aims and scope* [p. 2]: open-access, peer-reviewed journal covering all aspects of rare diseases and orphan drugs; "publishes high-quality reviews on specific rare diseases"; may also consider clinical-trial outcome reports (positive or negative) and public-health articles. **It does not accept case reports that do not present clinical or pathological findings informing the mechanisms of a disease; case reports that only present phenotype information "should not be considered."** Consequence: the paper must be framed as a systematic review on one rare disease, not as a case-series report; the own five patients are one component and their individual value is mechanism-neutral, so the paper stands on the review and the exposure-outcome analysis, not on the case descriptions.
- *Fees* [p. 4–5]: APC applies to accepted articles, price fixed on the acceptance date; current APC £2290 GBP / $3090 USD / €2590 EUR (plus VAT or local taxes where applicable). Discretionary waivers or discounts must be requested **at submission** (not later); automatic waivers/discounts for corresponding authors in the lowest-income countries (China is not stated as covered; not checked). Institutional open-access agreements may cover the fee (eligibility of the author's institution not checked).
- *Licence* [p. 5–6]: CC BY 4.0 (authors keep copyright); **CC0 1.0 data policy applies to all published data** in the journal. Permission from the rights holder is needed for previously published figures or tables in non-open-access journals (note in the legend and cite the source) [p. 12].
- *Submission* [p. 2, 18–19]: only an author may submit, not a third party; full names and e-mail addresses of **all co-authors** are required (authorship of the new second reviewer is still undecided). Cover letter must contain: why the manuscript belongs in the journal; any journal-policy issues; competing interests; confirmation that all authors approved the submission; confirmation that the content has not been published or submitted elsewhere (duplicate-publication policy); special-issue name if any. Reviewer suggestions optional (institutional e-mail, ORCID or Scopus ID; false names lead to rejection); non-preferred reviewers may also be named. Peer review is **single-anonymous** (reviewers know the authors; reports reach authors anonymously). Reviewers judge robustness, originality, clarity. Manuscript transfer within Springer Nature is offered after rejection (Transfer Desk).
- *Main text* [p. 6–7]: double-line spacing; **line and page numbering**; SI units, special characters embedded; **no page breaks**; DOC, DOCX, RTF or TeX/LaTeX (editable files required; no PDF-only submission).
- *Data and materials* [p. 8–9]: datasets that support the conclusions should be deposited in a public repository (where available and appropriate) or given in the paper or **as additional files, in machine-readable format (spreadsheet rather than PDF)**; an 'Availability of data and materials' section is required for all manuscripts, with the wordings "The dataset(s) supporting the conclusions of this article is(are) available in the [repository name] repository, [unique persistent identifier and hyperlink in https:// format]" or, if in additional files, "The dataset(s) supporting the conclusions of this article is(are) included within the article (and its additional file(s))."; public datasets must be cited in the reference list with an accession number or DOI (Force11 principles); software/code section content is listed (project name, home page, archived version DOI e.g. Zenodo, operating system, language, other requirements, licence, restrictions). The article-type page says whether data provision is mandatory (not seen).
- *Additional files* [p. 15–16]: **"Do not include any individual participant details"**; "data not shown" results must go in additional files; no links to personal or departmental websites; maximum 20 MB per file (virus-scanned); name them "Additional file 1" etc., cite each in sequence in the text, and list them in a separate manuscript section with file name, format (extension), title and description; no consent forms, language-editing certificates or tracked-change versions (send on request to the editorial office, quoting the manuscript number; do not send consent forms unless asked); tabular data as .xls or .csv. **Conflict to resolve before submission:** the protocol promises to share the full IPD dataset, but the guidelines forbid "individual participant details" in additional files. Not established whether a de-identified patient-level table (age band, sex, interval, outcome, source report) counts as "individual participant details"; ask the editorial office or read the BMC data-availability policy before deciding between a repository (Zenodo/OSF/Figshare with a DOI) and an additional file, and in either case de-identify the own five patients (no dates of birth, hospital numbers, images that identify).
- *Tables* [p. 14–15]: numbered in order of citation (Table 1, 2 …); a table shorter than one A4/Letter page stays in the text, longer ones go at the end of the text file with a note in the text; too-wide or very large tables go to additional files; **title above the table (max 15 words), legend below (max 300 words)**; made with the word processor's table object, not as pictures; **no colour or shading** (use superscripts, letters, symbols or bold, explained in the legend); **commas must not be used to indicate numerical values** — write 2548 and 4906, not 2,548 and 4,906 (the draft v0.1 uses commas and must be changed).
- *Figures* [p. 11–14]: numbered in order of first mention and uploaded in that order; multi-panel figures as one composite file; **title max 15 words, legend max 300 words, in the manuscript, not in the graphic file**; keys inside the graphic; each file at most 10 MB; formats EPS, PDF, Word or PowerPoint (single page), TIFF (LZW), JPEG (maximum quality), PNG, BMP; final PDF widths 85 mm (half page) or 170 mm (full page), maximum height 225 mm including the legend, about **300 dpi at final size**, lines wider than 0.25 pt, fonts embedded; web widths 600 px (standard) or 1200 px (high resolution); vectors preferably as PDF; crop closely. (The PRISMA flow diagram should be made as a vector figure; the own Case 5 images still need PACS re-export at 300 dpi.)
- *References* [p. 9–11]: only published or in-press articles, trial-registry records and abstracts, or items on public preprint servers; unpublished data and personal communications go in the text as "unpublished observations" / "personal communication" with the researchers' names and their permission, not in the list; only footnotes; **journal abbreviations follow Index Medicus/MEDLINE**; web links and URLs go in the reference list with a number, with site title, full URL and access date; link a group or author to a web link in the reference where possible. (A PROSPERO record can be cited as a registry record.)
- *Language* [p. 16–18]: help offered (native-speaker colleague, Springer Nature Author Services with a 15% first-use discount, free AJE AI language checker); not a requirement and no guarantee of acceptance. Springer Nature Author Services also translates from Spanish, Portuguese, Japanese or simplified Chinese into English.
- Promotion [p. 22–23] and Transfer Desk contact (transfersdesk@biomedcentral.com) are marginal.

**Consequences for the manuscript draft v0.1 (items 1 and 2 applied on 17 Oct 2026 — header now states what was and was not read, thousands separators removed in the .md and regenerated .docx, validate.py passed, 137 paragraphs, 0 comma-numbers left; items 3–9 not yet applied; note that `tools/md_to_docx.js` shades table header rows and therefore cannot produce the final submission tables as it is):** (1) remove the statement that the guidelines were unread, state which parts were read and which are still missing; (2) numbers without thousands separators in text and tables; (3) no colour or shading in tables (Table 1 and any later table); (4) table titles ≤15 words and legends ≤300 words, same for figures; (5) add line numbers and page numbers when the .docx is built for submission (the generator does not do this yet); (6) add an 'Availability of data and materials' section with the journal's exact wording once the dataset route is decided; (7) a cover letter covering the six items above (to be drafted later; the earlier EJP cover letter is a different journal's text); (8) the PRISMA flow diagram and Case 5 figures at the journal's resolution and size limits; (9) the Declarations list for this article type must be taken from the live Review/Research page, not guessed [done on 17 Oct 2026, see below].

**Research and Review pages read (17 Oct 2026; author uploaded `Research` 14 pp. and `Review` 13 pp., Springer Nature Link pages printed 2026/10/17 23:33; no text layer, rendered to images and read by Claude, all pages of both).** Both pages have the same Declarations, footnote and reference rules; they differ in the abstract, in what the body must contain and in the criteria.
- **Title page (both):** title includes the study design if appropriate (the Review page gives "…: A systematic review" as an example); full names and institutional addresses for all authors; a collaboration group may be listed as an author, with its members named in the Acknowledgements; **LLMs such as ChatGPT cannot be authors, and use of an LLM should be properly documented in the Methods section**; the corresponding author is indicated.
- **Research:** abstract **at most 350 words**, no references, abbreviations minimised, with the separate sections **Background, Results, Conclusions** (the page does not list Methods as an abstract section; the draft keeps a short Methods paragraph, which the page does not forbid); 3–10 keywords; main text Background (background, aims, summary of the existing literature, why the study was necessary), Methods (aim, design and setting; characteristics of participants or description of materials; a clear description of all processes, interventions and comparisons; type of statistical analysis incl. power calculation if appropriate), Results (findings incl. results of statistical analysis in text, tables or figures), Discussion (implications in the context of existing research; limitations), Conclusions (main conclusions and their importance and relevance to the field), List of abbreviations (can be provided), Declarations.
- **Review:** criteria: "comprehensive, authoritative descriptions of any subject within the scope of the journal"; abstract at most 350 words, structured with a background, a main body and a short conclusion; Background (aims, a summary of a search of the existing literature and the issue under discussion); Main text freely divided into subsections; Conclusions; **List of abbreviations must be provided**; Declarations. **No Methods or Results headings are prescribed for a Review.**
- **Recommended article type (Claude's reading, not the journal's statement):** Research, because the paper is a pre-registered analysis of pooled individual patient data with Methods and Results and own data; the Review description is narrative. The author may ask the editorial office; the editor can reclassify.
- **Declarations (both; all eight headings must appear, with "Not applicable" if irrelevant):** *Ethics approval and consent to participate* (statement even if approval was waived; name of the committee and its reference number if appropriate; animal studies need their own statement); *Consent for publication* (consent from the person or, for children, the parent or legal guardian for any individual details, images or videos; all case-report presentations need consent; institutional form or the journal's form; **do not send the form at submission, it may be requested at any stage including after publication**); *Availability of data and materials* (required statement; "minimal dataset necessary to interpret, replicate and build upon the findings"; when privacy could be compromised, still state availability and conditions; **authors are encouraged to preserve search strings on searchRxiv, which gives a DOI**; seven template statements — repository with persistent link; available from the corresponding author on reasonable request; all data in the article and its supplementary files; not publicly available due to a stated reason but available on reasonable request; data sharing not applicable; third-party data under licence; Not applicable; datasets cited in the reference list with a persistent identifier as a full URL, DataCite minimum information; optional co-submission of a data note to BMC Research Notes); *Competing interests* (all financial and non-financial; authors referred to by initials; if none: "The authors declare that they have no competing interests"); *Funding* (all sources; the funder's role in design, collection, analysis, decision to publish or preparation of the manuscript, if any); *Authors' contributions* (by initials; example ends "All authors read and approved the final manuscript."); *Acknowledgements* (anyone who contributed without meeting authorship criteria, incl. professional writing services; permission of everyone named; "Not applicable" if none; group-authorship rule); *Authors' information* (optional; initials; not for competing interests).
- **Footnotes and references (both):** footnotes, never endnotes; footnotes must not consist solely of a citation or contain bibliographic details, figures or tables; table footnotes use superscript lower-case letters (asterisks for significance values). **Vancouver style**, e.g. "Smith JJ. The world of science. Am J Sci. 1999;36:234-5.", with hyphens in page ranges; article by DOI "…Dig J Mol Med. 2000; doi:10.1007/…"; web links with site title, URL and "Accessed [date]"; dataset with persistent identifier "…GigaScience Database. 2011. http://dx.doi.org/10.5524/100012."
- **Stray text:** the last page of the Research PDF carries a heading "Tumor size" with rules on animal experiments and tumour burden — boilerplate, not applicable to this paper.
- **Applied to the manuscript draft the same day (v0.1, regenerated .docx):** header text rewritten (what was read, article-type recommendation, what is still not stated); new Methods subsection "Use of artificial intelligence tools" left PENDING with the two facts the statement must cover (the 10–11 October Codex-assisted screening decisions that were not used; Claude Code used for scripts, guides and the draft) — the authors decide the wording; new "List of abbreviations"; Declarations rewritten under the journal's headings with its wording (competing interests, authors' information, consent from parents or legal guardians, availability-of-data options and the open question whether a de-identified patient-level table counts as "individual participant details"); "Supplementary files" renamed "Additional files" with the journal's naming and listing rule ("Additional file 1" …); page ranges in the reference list now use hyphens. **Not done:** table shading and double spacing, line and page numbers in the .docx; the dataset route; the cover letter; the Methods paragraph on title and abstract screening (still PENDING for the screening-integrity reasons above).

(2) 'Deviations from the protocol': the PENDING bullet is replaced by two bullets (conflicts settled by a blind third reviewer plus checks of both-excluded records, added before he began; the 27 E4 safety-net records sought in full); (3) the AI-use subsection no longer mentions the Codex round; it holds a draft Claude Code statement in [PENDING] for the authors to confirm (who checked the AI-assisted work is for them to state; 'Rayyan's AI functions were not used' is the author's/protocol statement, not verified from the log); (4) Results > Study selection filled with verified numbers: both advanced 762; conflicts 734 (third reviewer advanced 248, excluded 486); targeted records judged 192, advanced 42 (21.9%); random sample 60, none advanced (one-sided 95% upper limit 4.9%); extension 616, advanced 12 (1.9%); 1064 to full text, 1484 excluded at title/abstract, +27 = 1091 sought in full text, of which 228 other-language listed not retrieved and 863 (695 English, 168 Chinese) to retrieve; (5) Limitations: the PENDING screening line replaced by a factual sentence on partial checking of both-excluded records and a single adjudicator. **Not written in the paper but still true and still to be disclosed elsewhere:** the 10–11 Oct Codex-assisted round, the 17 Oct entry pace (median 0.45 s per decision), the 250 rule-induced conflicts (the count is not in the paper; the rule itself is stated in one clause because it defines which records reached the third reviewer), and the no-abstract finding (39 of the 42 targeted records advanced were without abstract). **Claude's stated conditions (given to the author once):** the paper must not say the two reviewers screened independently by reading; the journal asks for any LLM use to be documented in the Methods, and the authors carry the responsibility for leaving the discarded AI-assisted round out of it (a one-sentence mention in the AI-use statement is recommended); the PROSPERO v1.5 amendment (separate document, still not submitted, v1.4 never submitted either) should state the facts; the Rayyan logs can be requested by the journal or reviewers. Everything else in the draft is unchanged.
