A change from Fei Peng (registered reviewer 2) to another person for the full-text stage is a deviation for protocol v1.5 / the PROSPERO amendment. (2) *Date rule:* the author intended to leave out old literature; Claude computed cut-offs on the 863 records then listed for retrieval (all have a year) and recommended 2000: 1990 → 661 to retrieve, **2000 → 540 (362 with abstract; English 439, Chinese 101; 323 dropped)**, 2005 → 440, 2010 → 352; of the 45 pilot IPD papers (76 children) a 2000 cut-off loses 1 paper (Hirose 1998, 1 child), 2005 loses 4 papers (11 children), 2010 loses 17 papers (29 children = 38%). Reasons given: it matches the registered structure (sensitivity analysis 7 'excluding reports published before 2000' and the publication-period covariate before 2000 / 2000–2014 / 2015 onward), it was fixed before any extraction so it cannot depend on outcomes, it costs almost no pilot IPD, and reports of that period mostly lack abstracts and are hard to retrieve. **Caveats stated to the author and written into the documents:** it is a deviation from the registered eligibility criteria (no date limit) and must go into protocol v1.5 / the PROSPERO amendment and the Methods deviations; the pilot set has nothing before 1998, so it cannot say what the pre-1998 literature contains; 'older data are worse' must NOT be claimed (no evidence), the rationale is feasibility; the effect on the results is unknown. (3) *Files changed:* `tools/make_fulltext_retrieval_list.py` (constant `CUTOFF_YEAR = 2000`; retrieval classes applied in this order: `Y published before 2000`, `L other language`, `retrieve`; also writes `fulltext_retrieval_summary.txt`), `search/screening/rescreen/fulltext_retrieval_list.csv` (regenerated; same 1091 records and columns, only `retrieval_class` changed for 485 records; the earlier version is in git history) and `fulltext_retrieval_summary.txt` (new). **New numbers (supersede 863 / 228 where the retrieval plan is meant):** 1091 on the list = **540 to retrieve** (English 439, Chinese 101; 362 with abstract, 178 without) + **485 `Y`** (English 256, Chinese 67, other languages 162; 189 with abstract, 296 without) + **66 `L`** (other languages published 2000 or later; 37 with abstract, 29 without); the 2000 boundary is inclusive (a record published in 2000 is sought; e.g. 李索林 2000 stays). `Full-text screening guide (draft).md/.docx` → **v2026-10-17a** (footer '全文筛选指南 v2026-10-17a'; validate.py passed, 270 paragraphs; no judgement rule (6.2–6.7, the 7 table rows) was changed except the added Y row. `Manuscript draft - systematic review with IPD (v0.1).md/.docx` regenerated (validate.py passed, 351 paragraphs, 0 comma-numbers): Selection of reports, a new Deviations bullet, Study selection numbers (485 / 66 / 540; 362 with abstract), the analysis paragraph (publication period will have two levels — PENDING for the statistician), Table 3 row 'before 2000' removed, Table 6 sensitivity analysis 7 marked 'not done', and a Limitations sentence. **Still to do because of this decision:** tell Jun Yang and the second full-text reviewer about the Y rule (the guide marks it); **18 Oct 2026 (early): AUTHOR DECISION — D1–D8 of the full-text guide accepted as Claude recommended, and D4 (tool) taken in the NEW form ("同意，D4也按新建议，整理决定表").** Decisions (all by the author alone; **Jun Yang and Hongqiang Bian have not yet confirmed**): **D1** paediatric 'splenic torsion' without position/ligament information → lenient, Include with `ws_basis` = Torsion only; exclude as E2 only if the authors state normal position with intact ligaments or another cause; a later sensitivity analysis dropping the flagged cases must be declared as an additional (not registered) analysis; 'adolescent/青少年' without an age → N. **D3** reason order Y → L → D → E5 → E4 → E1 → E2 → E3 → A → N → E6; same decision with different reasons is not a conflict. **D4 (new)** the full-text stage does NOT use Rayyan; the **FT_log sheet of the extraction workbook is the formal record**, one copy per reviewer (`FT_R1.xlsx`, `FT_R2.xlsx`, dated copies, no peeking until both finish), a new **FT_time_log** sheet (date, reviewer, start, end, reports handled, note) replaces Rayyan's time-stamped log, after the first 50 reports each reviewer gives the file to Jun Yang for safekeeping and a format/pace check (how the files reach Claude is for Jun Yang to decide; `review-for-refs` is written in the `note` column. **D5** 20-report calibration, both reviewers independently (≥3 Chinese, ≥2 reviews, ≥3 series, plus 2–3 D1-type), only agreement reported; the draw is to be done by Claude with a fixed seed (not done yet). **D6** second IPD e-mail four weeks after the first, then wait four more weeks, no reply = Exclude (A), at most 8 weeks from the first e-mail. **D7** report raw agreement, number of conflicts and kappa, no threshold. **D8** do not extract cases from review tables; **limited reference-list check** of the prior reviews listed in protocol A8 (minus Allen & Andrews 1989, excluded by the date rule), matched automatically against the 2,548 records, only unmatched references read; other `review-for-refs` reviews are not checked and the limitation is stated; how the reference lists can be exported and how many there are is NOT verified. **Files:** `Full-text screening guide (draft).md/.docx` (v2026-10-17a; **18 Oct 2026 (early): comparison script for the full-text logs written (author: "先写比对脚本").** New `tools/compare_fulltext_logs.py` (stdlib + openpyxl; `python3 -I` works). Two commands. **`check FT_R1.xlsx [--reviewer R1|R2] [--list PATH] [--max-per-hour 15]`** — one file only, safe for the midpoint check after the first 50 reports (shows nothing about the other reviewer): decision values (Include / Exclude / Pending IPD), reason names exactly as in the FT_log drop-down list, Exclude without a reason, Pending IPD with a reason other than A, Include with a reason, Include/Pending IPD with 'full text obtained = No', reason D without routes tried or with full text obtained, E6 without a note, Include without `ws_basis` or `n_children_individual`, non-integer child counts, duplicate WS rows (the later row is used), ids not on the retrieval list, **class Y records (published before 2000) that were logged**, class L records logged as something other than Exclude, reviewer column and dates (missing, future); from the FT_time_log sheet: sessions, hours, reports, minutes per report, **sessions above 15 reports per hour listed as 'to ask about' (a prompt for a question, not a finding; threshold is a parameter, chosen against the guide's crude 5-10 minutes per report)**, unreadable rows, end before start, FT_log decisions against FT_time_log reports (total and per date). Repeated warnings are grouped into one line with the row numbers. **`compare FT_R1.xlsx FT_R2.xlsx --out DIR [--list PATH] [--aggregate-only]`** — run only when both reviewers have finished; matches by WS id; categories: agree Include / Exclude / Pending IPD, conflict (any decision difference), only in one file; agreement and Cohen's kappa for 3 categories and for Exclude vs not-Exclude (rough 95% CI by the same simple formula as the calibration; a note says that below 50 records only raw agreement should be reported, guide D5), contingency table, reason agreement among both-Exclude pairs, per-reviewer counts and Exclude reasons, retrieval-list coverage, pace for both files; files written: `ft_compare_summary.txt`, `ft_compare_all.csv`, `ft_conflicts_for_discussion.csv` (title, both reviewers' decision/reason/ws_basis/child counts/note, empty columns for the discussion, the adjudicator and the final decision), `ft_detail_differences.csv` (both Include but different `ws_basis` / `n_children_individual` / `n_children_aggregate` / `duplicate_of`; both Pending IPD but different aggregate counts; both Exclude but different reason, with the reason proposed by the D3 order L, D, E5, E4, E1, E2, E3, A, N, E6). `--aggregate-only` prints counts only (no WS ids, no files). **Claude's reading, flagged in the guide and for the three to confirm: Include vs Pending IPD counts as a conflict** (the guide names only Include vs Exclude and Pending IPD vs Exclude, but section 10 says every decision decides whether a report enters extraction). **Test (synthetic data only, scratchpad, nothing committed):** two invented FT_log workbooks built on the real template and real WS ids with every case (same decision, different details, different reasons, each kind of conflict, a record in one file only, format errors, duplicate rows, a class-Y record, an id not on the list, a fast session, end before start); kappa (0.575), agreement (75.8%), the contingency table, the reason agreement (8 of 12) and the categories were recomputed independently and matched; the aggregate-only output contains no WS id; empty files do not crash. Not done: a `finalize` step that reads the filled conflict file and writes the final Include / Pending IPD lists and the PRISMA counts per reason; the FT_log time stamp is only a date plus the FT_time_log sessions, so pace can be checked per session, not per report. Guide (`Full-text screening guide (draft).md/.docx`, still v2026-10-17a): sections 3, 10 and 12 now name the script commands, the output folder for filled lists is `search/screening/fulltext/`; validate.py passed, 282 paragraphs, all 279 text segments of the .md found in the .docx.

**18 Oct 2026 (early): the 20 calibration reports for the full-text stage are drawn (author: "先抽那20篇校准报告").** New script `tools/draw_ft_calibration.py` (seed **20261018**, reproducible and independent of the Python version: within each stratum records are ranked by sha256(SEED|stratum|ws_id), final order by sha256(SEED|order|ws_id); run twice, identical output). Population: the 540 records of `fulltext_retrieval_list.csv` with `retrieval_class = retrieve`. Required minimum (guide D5): 3 D1-type, 2 review, 3 Chinese, 3 series, drawn in that order (a record already drawn counts toward every stratum it belongs to), the rest random. **Strata are keyword proxies, not judgements:** chinese = language group; review = title with review / 综述 / 进展 / overview and none of case / report / series / 例 (pool 5; 'case report and literature review' titles are deliberately not in it); series = English title with series / cohort / retrospective / multicentre / experience / cases, Chinese title with N例 (N ≥ 10 or 三–十) / 回顾 / 病例分析 (pool 26); d1 = torsion or volvulus word + child cue + no wandering/ectopic/floating/migrating/mobile/movable/pelvic-spleen/ptosis word in title or abstract (pool 23); Chinese pool 101. **Result:** 20 drawn, 8 by stratum and 12 random; in the 20 (overlaps possible) Chinese 8, review 2, series 3, D1-type 5; 16 have an abstract; English 12, Chinese 8; **1 is one of the 53 pilot papers** (the 李索林 2000 series, WS2145; Jun Shu knows the pilot papers — not changed; a redraw excluding pilot papers is possible if the author prefers). Files in `search/screening/fulltext/`: `calibration_20_list.csv` (for BOTH reviewers: position, ws_id, year, language, has_abstract, title, doi, url — no strata and no 'source' column, because the retrieval list's source would reveal how a record reached the full-text stage), `calibration_20_key.csv` (strata flags and pilot flag — **not for the reviewers, closed until both have finished**) and `calibration_20_design.txt`. Claude gave no opinion on any record's inclusion/exclusion before or after the draw and must not. The guide section 12 now states the draw (still v2026-10-17a; validate.py passed). **Next:** the three confirm the decision sheet (Jun Yang and Hongqiang Bian have not yet); each reviewer fills their own FT_log (a pre-filled FT_log with these 20 ws_ids per reviewer is NOT made yet — offered), retrieves the 20 full texts, logs times in FT_time_log; then `compare_fulltext_logs.py compare` and a discussion of the differences; the 20 are processed again in the formal screening.

**18 Oct 2026 (later still): `finalize` script for the full-text stage written, and the guides/manual brought up to protocol v1.5 (author: "同意，先写 finalize 脚本和对齐稿件").** New `tools/finalize_fulltext.py` (stdlib + openpyxl; loads `tools/compare_fulltext_logs.py` by file path, so it works under `python3 -I`): `python3 -I tools/finalize_fulltext.py FT_R1.xlsx FT_R2.xlsx --conflicts <filled ft_conflicts_for_discussion.csv> [--ipd <extraction workbook>] [--out DIR] [--list PATH]`. **Rules (all Claude's operationalisation of guide sections 8, 10, 11; the three have not seen them):** same decision → that decision; both Exclude with different reasons → first applicable in the D3 order (exception: exactly one D = conflict); a conflict needs a row in the filled conflict list and takes `final_decision`, else `adjudicator_decision`, else `discussion_agreed_decision` (the fall-backs are listed as notes and `resolved_by` says which was used); a final Exclude needs a reason (final/adjudicator/discussion reason column, else the reason the reviewers gave); a conflict missing from the filled list, without a decision, records handled by one reviewer only, and invalid decisions are UNRESOLVED and counted nowhere; a filled-list row that is no conflict any more is reported as stale; class-Y records found in a log are ignored with a note. With `--ipd`, the IPD_requests sheet decides Pending IPD: `Included with IPD` → Include, `Narrative only` → Exclude (A), `Not eligible after check` → unresolved, `Pending`/no row → stays pending. Details for included records (`ws_basis`, child counts, duplicate link): filled only when both reviewers included and agree, or taken from the one reviewer who included (flagged), left blank when they differ; IPD-received includes carry no child count from this stage. **Outputs** (default `search/screening/fulltext/final/`): `fulltext_final_list.csv` only when nothing is unresolved and every record on the retrieval list marked `retrieve` has been handled — otherwise `fulltext_provisional_list.csv` and a PROVISIONAL status line (only one of the two names can exist, the other is deleted); `fulltext_included.csv`, `fulltext_pending_ipd.csv`, `fulltext_unresolved.csv`, `fulltext_prisma_counts.txt` (Y, L, to retrieve, handled, D, assessed, excluded by reason, Pending IPD, included reports and children, linked duplicates, how conflicts were settled, E4 safety-net outcomes, IPD request counts, notes). **Tested on synthetic data only** (scratchpad, 20 real calibration WS ids with invented decisions covering every branch: agreement, detail differences, different Exclude reasons, both D, D conflict, each way of filling the conflict list, stale row, undecided conflict, one reviewer only, three IPD outcomes, a logged class-Y record); every count was checked by hand against the scenario table; a complete scenario gives FINAL, a missing conflict list gives PROVISIONAL. **Never run on real logs yet.** **Documents aligned:** `Full-text screening guide (draft)` → v2026-10-18d (protocol v1.5; step 7 of section 2 and a new bullet in section 10 describe the script; no judgement rule changed); `Screening guide - title and abstract (draft)` → v2026-10-18a (protocol v1.5 in the title and two statements that are still true in v1.5; new "状态" paragraph: the stage was completed on 17 Oct as described in protocol v1.5 Section 14 and Appendix D, and **section 12 (the plan with 雷海燕 as second reviewer) was not carried out**; revision paragraph in section 11; no criterion, 4.3 row or reason name changed); `Screening manual - step by step (draft)` → v2026-10-18a (protocol v1.5 wherever v1.3 was cited, guide version references, a note at the top of chapter 11 that the full-text guide supersedes it). The three .docx files were regenerated and validate.py passed (288 / 284 / 1,131 paragraphs); every text segment of the full-text and title/abstract guides is in the .docx (249 / 241 of 249 / 241); for the manual the only segments not found are markup lines (callout markers, page breaks, the image line); layouts not seen in Word.

Case 1/2 `idx_dur_lower_bound` = Yes (rule 9, no time to operation), Case 4 No (emergency laparotomy adds nothing); Case 5 rules `1;8` (18 h + 2.5 h = 20.5 h = 0.9 d); Case 3 fixation = Direct suture (capsular sutures to diaphragm and peritoneum with clips after an abandoned pouch), Case 4 = Other (specify) (reinforced two-pole full-thickness fixation with haemostatic gauze); imaging columns pooled over all encounters (guide silent); `year` = 2026 placeholder only so `pub_period` can be derived (statistician to decide the period for the own series); `consecutive` = Unclear until the identification method is documented; `waiting_interval_days` and `total_hist_days` are approximations (stated months x 30.4 plus 1–2 symptom days: 486.4/488.4, 577.6/579.6, 1884.8/1885.8) to be replaced by exact intervals from dates. **Items the file flags for the records (Chart_check sheet, 22 rows):** exact onset/operation times (Cases 1, 2, 4); Case 2 `ws_basis` (Table 1 says only 'CT: pedicle torsion'); Case 3 worsening period within the nine days, extent of infarction and histology, torsion at operation, whether the spleen removed at the second operation was viable; **Case 5: the admission to another hospital one week before presentation — if the abdominal symptoms were continuous over that week the index episode is about 7 days, not 0.9 (the draft keeps 0.9 and leaves `total_hist_days` and `n_earlier_episodes` blank)**; follow-up length for Cases 1–3 (not in the manuscript) and updates for Cases 4 and 5; vaccination and prophylaxis after splenectomy. **How it is to be used (stated in the file's README and in extraction guide section 3, row 'OWN'):** E1 and E2 extract the five children from the records themselves, without looking at the draft or at each other's file; the draft is compared with the consensus afterwards, which tests the manuscript against the records. Starting from the draft and checking it cell by cell is possible but then the five children are no longer independently double-extracted, which would have to be stated in the Methods — **the choice between the two is for the three to make before they start; not decided.** `Extraction guide - how to fill the extraction workbook (draft).md/.docx` got one added sentence in that table row (version label unchanged; validate.py passed, 652 paragraphs; all text segments present except one blockquote marker). The draft was never opened in Excel or WPS; data validations (46 on Patients, 7 on Reports) and formulas were checked to be present after the openpyxl round trip.

**19 Oct 2026: PLANNING LIST OF POSSIBLE IPD TARGETS (author: "先写 IPD 邮件的目标清单").** The real target list does not exist yet: a report becomes an IPD target only when both full-text screeners record Pending IPD (reason A) and the conflicts are settled (`tools/finalize_fulltext.py` writes `fulltext_pending_ipd.csv`; the IPD_requests sheet of the extraction workbook then tracks the e-mails). What was made is a **candidate list for planning the size of the batch**, built before any full-text decision: `tools/make_ipd_target_candidates.py` (`python3 -I tools/make_ipd_target_candidates.py search/ipd`) writes `search/ipd/ipd_target_candidates.xlsx` and `.csv` (36 of the 540 reports to retrieve). **Tiers:** A 4 = pilot papers read in full on 28 Sep 2026 and found aggregate-only or partly aggregate (Rescorla 2007 WS0616, 2 wandering-spleen patients inside a 231-child laparoscopic series; Fiquet-Francois 2010 WS0550, 14 patients, individual data only for 8 preserved ones without intervals; Lombardi 2014 WS0449, 3 cases plus an imaging review of 55 children; Cohen 2018 WS0321, five children listed in the pilot file, total and per-child detail not recorded — this one is on the list only through the E4 safety net); B 26 = title/abstract states 3 or more patients (number found by regular expressions; numbers in literature contexts such as 'less than 500 cases reported', 'additional 127 cases' are excluded and listed separately; includes the two known Chinese series 李拴玲 2024, 19 children, WS1859, and Zhao 2025, 6 children, WS0049); C 3 = series/cohort/retrospective wording without a number; R 3 = review wording (Ganarin 2021, a pictorial review, a Chinese review), not targets unless they report own patients. Zivkovic 1998 (6 patients, aggregate-only) is NOT on it because the date rule excludes it (class Y). **Limits stated in the README sheet:** numbers come from keywords/regex on title and abstract, none checked one by one; the 178 reports without abstract are covered only through the title; an abstract number says nothing about whether per-patient data are given (a report with a patient table is not a target); tier A is Claude's single-reader judgement from the pilot, not a screeners' decision. **Rules around it:** the screeners should not open the list while they screen (it hints at what a report looks like) — it is for the sender of the e-mails (Jun Shu is also screener R1, so he should consult it only after his own full-text decisions are in, or leave it to 杨俊); no e-mail address is written in it (columns for the corresponding author's name and where the address was found are empty); the protocol timeline (first e-mail, reminder after 4 weeks, final status within 8 weeks) is unchanged. `IPD request emails (draft).md/.docx` section 6 got a pointer to the list (footer unchanged; validate.py passed). Not done: no e-mail was sent, no corresponding author was looked up, no full text of any candidate was read in this step.

**19 Oct 2026: the two Chinese series (赵娜 2025 WS0049, 李拴玲 2024 WS1859) are NOT dropped in advance (author asked "赵娜和李拴玲的可以不要吗？", then gave the reason "担心联系不上作者").** Claude separated three meanings and advised against each form of dropping: removing them from the planning list changes nothing (the list is non-binding); skipping their IPD request would be a deviation from protocol v1.5 Section 14 and the published PROSPERO v2.0 (authors contacted: Yes) and would need a stated reason in the Methods and in the record (Additional information is a minor-amendment field but has little room left, about 30 words by Claude's count, not verified); excluding them from the review is not allowed (eligibility is decided at full text; if aggregate-only and unanswered they end as Exclude, reason A, described narratively). Facts from the RIS abstracts only: 赵娜, Zhonghua Yi Xue Za Zhi 2025;105(29):2507-10, Tianjin Children's Hospital, 6 children, all splenectomy; 李拴玲, Fa Yu Yi Xue Dian Zi Za Zhi 2024;12(5):369-73, Beijing Children's Hospital, 19 children, 14 splenectomies; neither abstract says whether a per-patient table exists or whether the cases are wandering spleen. Reason given: worry that the authors cannot be reached. Answer: that is not a reason to skip — an unanswered request costs one e-mail and ends as a counted 'no response' (reason A, narrative description), while a reachable author may supply 25 children's data; contact routes are for the sender to try in this order: the address printed in the paper (never guess it, never write it into the repo), the institution's public page, the journal's editorial office as forwarder, and a colleague who knows the department (any extra route is recorded in IPD_requests and mentioned in one sentence in the Methods). Nothing was changed in the files; the planning list keeps both rows; the real decision is made after the two full-text screeners have read the full texts.

**19 Oct 2026 (later still): `finalize_fulltext.py --ipd` now reports the contact figures (author: "同意，让 finalize 统计联系不上的作者数").** New in `tools/finalize_fulltext.py`: `read_ipd` also reads `corresponding_author`, `delivery_result`, `other_routes`, `author_reached` and `contact_log` (optional — an older template without them still runs and a note says the contact figures are unavailable), and `contact_summary` adds to the IPD REQUESTS block of `fulltext_prisma_counts.txt`: first-e-mail results (sent no bounce / bounced / no address found / not yet sent / blank), `author_reached` counts (yes / no / pending / blank), authors requested and authors not reached by any route (with the number of reports), and the routes used besides the address in the paper (institution page / journal editorial office / colleague / other, counted by report). **Counting rules (Claude's, stated in the script):** only reports with a first-e-mail date count as requested; one author is counted once when the name is written identically (case and extra spaces ignored — 'Li S' and 'Li Shuanling' would count as two, so the names should be written the same way in the sheet); a report without an author name counts as its own author and a note says so. **Notes on contradictory entries:** 'No' without a first-e-mail date; 'No' with a reply date or received data; 'No' with no other route recorded; 'No' while the final status is still Pending; 'Yes' with neither a reply date nor a contact-log line; a bounced first e-mail with no other route recorded and not reached; a ws_id in more than one row (the later row is used, as before — a blank duplicate row would therefore overwrite a filled one, which is why the note is there). **Tests (invented IPD sheets on real WS ids, scratchpad only; nothing saved):** seven rows chosen so that every figure could be counted by hand (5 requested, 2 replied, 1 data received; first e-mail 3 sent / 2 bounced; reached 2 yes / 3 no; 4 authors of whom 2 not reached, 3 reports — 'Li S' and '  li   s ' counted once; routes Institution page 2, Journal editorial office 2, Colleague 2, Other 1); the script output matched on every figure and gave the expected eight notes; the old template (no contact columns) gives the single 'older template' note and no crash; **19 Oct 2026 (later still): 4-week and 8-week deadline check added to `finalize_fulltext.py --ipd` (author: "同意，加上 8 周到期的提醒").** New option `--today yyyy-mm-dd` (default: the run date; give it to repeat a run; an invalid date stops the script) and function `deadline_check`. For every request with a readable first-e-mail date and an open final status (Pending or blank): after 28 days with no second-e-mail date, no reply date and no data received it flags 'send the reminder (week 4)'; after 56 days it flags 'the 8 weeks are over and the final status is still Pending - settle it (Included with IPD / Narrative only / Not eligible after check)' and adds whether 'Author reached' is still Pending or blank. The IPD REQUESTS block of `fulltext_prisma_counts.txt` gets two lines: the date of the check with the number of requests past week 4 without a second e-mail and past week 8 still Pending, and the number of open requests still inside their 8 weeks with the earliest 8-week deadline and its WS id. Further notes: an unreadable date in `email1_date`, `email2_date` or `reply_date` (that entry is skipped), a first-e-mail date after the run date, a second e-mail dated before the first. Closed requests (Included with IPD, Narrative only, Not eligible after check) are never flagged. **The check happens only when the script is run - nothing sends a reminder by itself - so the e-mail draft says to run it about weekly after the first e-mails go out.** The deadline is counted from the first e-mail (56 days), as in guide D6. **Test (invented IPD sheet, eleven requests with dates chosen against a fixed run date 2026-12-31, scratchpad only):** every figure was counted by hand beforehand and matched - 2 past week 4 without a second e-mail (one of them also past week 8), 2 past week 8 still Pending (a blank status counts as open), 5 open within their 8 weeks with the earliest deadline 2027-01-15, closed requests with old dates not flagged, a date typed as text '2026/11/02' read correctly, the three date problems noted; earlier test sheets, the older template without contact columns, an empty IPD sheet, a run without `--ipd` and an invalid `--today` behave as before or stop with a clear message. **Not done:** the thresholds (28 and 56 days) are fixed in the script, not parameters; never run on real logs. `IPD request emails (draft).md/.docx` -> v2026-10-19d (section 1 timetable bullet and section 5 mention the check; validate.py passed, 123 paragraphs, all 120 text segments present) and `Full-text screening guide (draft).md/.docx` -> v2026-10-19c (status-only: one clause in the finalize paragraph of section 10, a change note; validate.py passed, 292 paragraphs; the single segment flagged by the crude text test, D7, is present and is a bold-run artefact of the test); layouts not seen in Word.

**19 Oct 2026 (later still): the 4-week and 8-week periods of the IPD deadline check are now parameters (author: "同意，把期限做成可调参数").** `tools/finalize_fulltext.py` has two new options, `--reminder-days N` (default 28: the second e-mail is due) and `--limit-days N` (default 56: the final status must be settled), with the check `0 < reminder-days < limit-days` (otherwise the script stops with a message; non-integers are refused by the argument parser). Messages and the counts file now say the number of days actually used instead of 'week 4' / '8 weeks': the line 'Deadlines as of <date> (reminder point day N, limit day M, both counted from the first e-mail)' records the values of that run, so a saved counts file shows which periods were applied. **The defaults are the registered ones (guide D6, protocol Section 14: second e-mail after four weeks, final status within eight weeks of the first e-mail); changing them is a departure from the registered plan and must be stated in the Methods** - the guide and the e-mail draft say so. **Test (the same invented eleven-request sheet and fixed run date 2026-12-31 as before, scratchpad only):** with the defaults the result is unchanged (2 past the reminder point, 2 past the limit, 5 still inside, earliest limit date 2027-01-15 WS0100); with `--reminder-days 14 --limit-days 42` the figures counted by hand beforehand were matched (3 past the reminder point, 2 past the limit, 5 still inside, earliest limit date 2027-01-01 WS0100); `--reminder-days 60 --limit-days 56`, `--reminder-days 0` and `--limit-days abc` stop with a clear message. `IPD request emails (draft).md/.docx` -> v2026-10-19e (section 1 timetable bullet names the two options and the 28/56-day defaults; validate.py passed, 123 paragraphs, all 120 text segments present) and `Full-text screening guide (draft).md/.docx` -> v2026-10-19d (status-only: a clause in the finalize paragraph of section 10, a change note; validate.py passed, 293 paragraphs; the single segment flagged by the crude text test, D7, is present and is a bold-run artefact of the test); layouts not seen in Word. Never run on real logs.

De-identified outputs of `python3 -I tools/compare_fulltext_logs.py compare R1 R2 --out … --list search/screening/rescreen/fulltext_retrieval_list.csv` are in `search/screening/fulltext/calibration_results/` (`ft_compare_summary.txt`, `ft_compare_all.csv`, `ft_conflicts_for_discussion.csv`, `ft_detail_differences.csv`; grep for e-mail patterns: 0). **Format check:** R1 FT_log 0 errors, 0 warnings, no usable FT_time_log session at all; R2 FT_log 0 errors, but FT_time_log gives 4 warnings (row 44 unreadable; one session of 35 min with 16 reports = 27/h, above the 15/h prompt; 20 decisions in FT_log vs 16 reports in the time log). **Result (raw agreement only, as D5 says):** 20 of 20 records in both files; agree Include 13, agree Exclude 5, **2 conflicts (both Include in R1 vs Pending IPD in R2): WS0049 (赵娜 2025, 6 children) and WS1859 (李拴玲 2024, 19 children) — the two Chinese series on the IPD planning list**; 3-category agreement 18/20 = 90.0% (kappa 0.78 printed by the script, not to be reported at n = 20); Exclude vs not-Exclude 100%. Both-Exclude 5, same reason 4, different reason 1 (WS0018: R1 E3, R2 E4; the D3 order proposes E4, so by the guide it is not a conflict). ### PROSPERO readiness check and extraction-stage decision (28 Sep 2026)
- Web search only (official crd.york.ac.uk / ojrd.biomedcentral.com / link.springer.com pages are blocked by the container's network proxy, so nothing was read directly — treat all PROSPERO/OJRD procedural claims below as needing the author's own verification on the live site): reviews that have already started data extraction at initial submission are not eligible for PROSPERO; piloting an extraction form is expected and allowed. Since the Feb 2025 platform relaunch, submissions go through automated completeness/eligibility/duplicate checks, and a record is **not published until every listed team member individually approves it via an emailed confirmation link** — this is usually the rate-limiting step, not CRD review; turnaround is typically days once everyone has clicked through.
- Risk flagged: the 53-paper pilot went beyond piloting a form (exposure + outcome extracted for 76 children, loss rates by interval examined, and this shaped the choice of primary exposure).
- **Decided with the author: tick "data extraction: not started" on PROSPERO AND paste the protocol's "Pilot work before registration" paragraph (section 1) into a free-text field of the PROSPERO form itself**, not just leave it in the Word file. Rationale: the formal dual-reviewer extraction on the systematic search has not started; the pilot is disclosed in the same breath; the pilot data already exist in this repo and must be reported in the manuscript under PRISMA regardless. Fallback if PROSPERO rejects or queries the record: register the same protocol on OSF Registries instead.
- Author will need every review-team member's email address on the PROSPERO form for the approval-link step (Jun Shu, Jun Yang, Hongqiang Bian, Fei Peng, Ji Wang) — only Jun Shu's and Jun Yang's are recorded here (see below); the other three are not yet in this repo.

### PROSPERO record created and submitted (28 Sep 2026)
Jun Shu completed the full PROSPERO registration form (all sections: title/basic info, eligibility criteria, similar-reviews check against Ganarin 2021 CRD42018089971 and two unrelated matches, timetable 28 Sep 2026 → 28 Jun 2027, full protocol uploaded as PDF, search and selection, data collection/IPD, outcomes, data synthesis, current stage of review — pilot work marked Complete with the "Pilot work before registration" paragraph placed in Additional information since no inline text box appeared, risk of bias via Murad 2018 [listed as "Other" since not in PROSPERO's standard tool list], certainty via GRADE, publishing plans in English, peer review: not peer reviewed, MeSH terms). Record status as of this session: **"New (publishing)"** on the My PROSPERO dashboard — submitted but not yet issued a CRD number; publication is pending every listed team member clicking their emailed confirmation link. **Confirmed by the author (28 Sep 2026): all 5 team members (Jun Shu, Jun Yang, Hongqiang Bian, Fei Peng, Ji Wang) are added to the record and have received PROSPERO's confirmation email.** (Jun Shu himself did not get a separate confirmation email — as the account holder who created and submitted the record, his agreement is implicit; only the other 4 needed to click through.)

**REGISTERED. PROSPERO registration number: CRD420261520329.** Record status "Registered records published and stable" as of 28 Sep 2026. Use this number in the manuscript's Methods section (“This systematic review was registered on PROSPERO (CRD420261520329)”) and in the cover letter to Orphanet Journal of Rare Diseases.
